## Real, significant bug found + fixed after the restart: `child_ids` never persisted across restarts — root cause of jurisdiction's duplicate registrations AND every structural traversal mode's broken parent→child walk

The operator restarted (picking up task 56 + task 54's scoping work). Boot log showed `Jurisdiction store: 41 new registrations (0 scopes already registered)` — a line that should not show 0-already-registered this far into the session. Traced the specific field end to end (method 25 below) instead of assuming it was benign.

- **Root cause** (`src/zsei/storage.rs`): `GlobalState.child_ids` (a variable-length list) was never written into the fixed-width mmap header record at all. `store_global` correctly persists `container_id`/`child_count`/`version`/`parent_id` in-place, but `child_ids` only ever lived in `child_ids_cache: HashMap<ContainerID, Vec<ContainerID>>` — an in-memory field populated solely by the current process's own `store()` calls, starting empty on every `ContainerStorage::new()`. Every restart silently reset every container's child_ids to empty.
- **Confirmed on disk, not just inferred**: counted `JurisdictionRuleSet` containers directly in `target/release/zsei_data/local/*.json` — **299 total for ~41 real unique scopes**. Nearly every scope (global, eu, us, gb, cn, jp, ...) has exactly 9 duplicate containers, matching roughly 9 restarts across tonight's session. The self-heal registration logic itself (`src/lib.rs`) was always correct — the bug was entirely in the durability of the field `already_registered` read.
- **Bigger implication, also confirmed via grep**: `ContainerStorage::get_children()` reads the exact same broken field, and every "Structural" traversal mode (`src/zsei/traversal.rs`) uses it for parent→child walking. So structural (non-relation-edge) graph traversal has been silently a no-op for anything not freshly created in the current session, after every restart — not introduced by today's task-56 traversal wiring, but directly relevant to it (the new relation-edge-following half works fine, since `Context.relationships` lives in `local_state`, which IS correctly persisted — only the structural/parent-child half was broken).
- **Fixed**: added `rebuild_child_ids_cache()`, called right after `load_index()` in `ContainerStorage::new()`. Since `parent_id` is correctly persisted per-container, one pass over every already-indexed container reading its parent_id back and grouping by it fully reconstructs `child_ids_cache` from genuinely durable state — no storage-format change needed.
- **Built clean twice, independently verified**: first in isolation (real exit 0), then again combined with an unrelated methodology-index edit made mid-build (real exit 0, binary mtime confirmed newer than every touched source file).
- **Not yet done, flagged honestly**: the 258+ pre-existing duplicate `JurisdictionRuleSet` containers already on disk are not cleaned up by this fix — it only stops further duplication going forward. Real cleanup task, lower priority than the root-cause fix, queued separately. Also relevant to ZCode's earlier "KNOWN QUIRK: root child_count field lags on reload — cosmetic, queued" note (related but distinct — `child_count` itself persists correctly via in-place header overwrite; only the `child_ids` list was the confirmed non-durable field) — not yet re-checked whether that quirk is now also resolved as a side effect.
- **5 new real methodologies added (22-26)**, drawn directly from this investigation: 22 (Persistent-vs-Cached Field Parity), 23 (Idempotency Checks Must Read Durable State), 24 (Incremental Layering Requires Reconciliation — ties this bug and the UI fork's duplicate-CSS finding together as the same bug class), 25 (Trace the Full Write-to-Read Path — the debugging technique that found this), 26 (Attribution Requires a Capability Check — from catching my own graph-wiring fork's incorrect guess about who fixed its missing struct field). Mirrored into `target/release/zsei_data/methodologies/`, `bootstrap.rs` default index updated to 26. Pending next restart to register as live ZSEI containers.

## Real cross-relationship linking built for text-modality graphs (fork, per /btw directive) — live-verified end to end, including traversal

User directive: every file type we accept should be auto-graphed (confirmed already true via a prior investigation fork), and those graphs need real cross-relationships so they interconnect with the whole ZSEI graph — that IS this project's abstract protocol for passing context, not a flat keyword-scan dump.

**Confirmed gap** (investigation fork, no code changes): `detect_file_modality()` (src/orchestrator/graphs.rs:551) auto-routes ~60 extensions across ~25 modalities and creates a real per-file graph automatically on every request — genuinely live. But `src/orchestrator/mod.rs:1765` hardcoded `link_to_existing: false`, and even flipped, both text (`main.rs:5572`) and code (`main.rs:1284`) modality `create_graph` handlers ignored the parameter entirely (`_link_to_existing: bool`, leading underscore, unused). `ZSEIQuery::LinkFile` exists but has zero real call sites and is the wrong shape (`{project_id, file_path}`, not container-to-container).

**Built** (text modality; code modality deliberately left open, see below):
1. Flipped the flag at the orchestrator call site.
2. `persist_graph_container` now returns its own real derived keywords/topics alongside the container id (not a separate re-derivation — linking reflects exactly what the container advertises about itself).
3. New `link_related_containers()`: type-blind `SearchContainersByKeywords` (the search primitive only supports one type filter, too narrow for "find anything genuinely related across any content type"), filtered client-side using the exact same `is_infrastructure_container_type` exclusion list `context_aggregation`'s earlier real fix established — reused, not reinvented. Requires ≥2 shared keyword/topic terms (documented threshold reasoning: one shared term is often coincidental even post the length≥3+stopword search fix, two independently-shared terms is meaningfully stronger). Writes real bidirectional `Relation` edges (`RelationType::SimilarTo`, `DiscoveryMethod::TextAnalysis` — correct auto-discovery provenance per methodology 26, not `Manual`) via `UpdateContainer` on both sides, idempotent.

**Live-verified, not just compiled** (both the pipeline crate and main workspace built clean, real exit 0 each): ran the real pipeline binary 3x directly against the live host with crafted content — two graphs sharing 2 real signals ("transformer" keyword + "neural network architecture" topic) correctly got bidirectional `SimilarTo` edges (confidence scaling 0.6→0.75 with overlap count); a third, deliberately unrelated graph (castle/medieval content, zero shared terms) correctly got zero edges. Verified via independent `GetContainer` reads on every container involved, not trusted from the pipeline's own log line alone. Then ran a real `ZSEIQuery::Traverse` (Structural mode) from the first container and confirmed it actually reaches both linked containers through the new edges — this project already had one case tonight (jurisdiction) where edges were written correctly but traversal was separately broken by an unrelated bug; didn't assume this one was different without checking, and it wasn't broken.

**Honestly still open**: code modality untouched (explicit stretch goal, not rushed — a well-verified single-modality implementation beats a half-tested spread). Other ~23 modalities untouched. Requires a host restart for the orchestrator-side flip to take effect on real requests (lives in the main binary); the pipeline-side logic is already live via the standalone binary regardless. Test containers (30292-30295) are live on the running host — harmless (clearly fake content, not linked to real user data) but not cleaned up, flagged rather than risking a rushed delete.

1 new real methodology (27: Broad Relevance Discovery Pattern) — the type-blind-search-plus-client-side-filter pattern this work reused, made explicit for whoever implements the same linking for the next modality. Mirrored into `target/release/zsei_data/methodologies/`, `bootstrap.rs` index updated to 27, build confirmed clean.

## Task 58 (fork): jurisdiction graph test block T-J1..T-J5 — all 5 built, all green, real bugs and a real test-writing mistake both caught

Completing task 58's jurisdiction half (`docs/GRAPH_TEST_PLAN.md` section 1). Two small, safe, behavior-preserving extractions made the boot-time self-heal logic independently testable rather than writing shallow tests around the untestable inline block:

- **`compute_new_jurisdiction_registrations`** (`src/lib.rs`) — the already-registered/new-candidate decision logic pulled out of the registration block. T-J1 calls it twice (empty already-registered set, then a full one) and asserts 0 new on the second call — directly codifying the exact live bug this session found and fixed (child_ids not persisting made every restart show "N new / 0 already-registered" instead of the correct steady state).
- **`compute_jurisdiction_edges_to_add`** (`src/lib.rs`) — the relationship-edge decision logic (global edge for every non-global scope, additional eu edge for EU members) pulled out the same way. One function proves both **T-J2**'s edge-shape claim and **T-J5**'s idempotency claim (second call with the first call's edges already merged in returns zero new edges).
- **T-J2** also built a real, on-disk `ContainerStorage` + `TraversalEngine` fixture (not a mock — this is testing the traversal mechanism itself) with a national→eu→global relation chain plus a same-shape distractor of a different container type, proving the traversal both reaches the baseline through real edges AND that the type filter genuinely excludes non-matching containers, not just that BFS happens to work.
- **T-J3** split across two files by necessity: `HardwareRegionSignals::agreed_region` (already a pure, directly-testable method — no refactor needed) got 4 fixture cases in `jurisdiction.rs`; the "explicit config always wins" behavior needed a test inside `src/config/mod.rs` itself since `apply_hardware_region_detection` is a private method not reachable from another module's test.
- **T-J4** walks the real shipped `assets/jurisdiction/` files (41 of them), not a fixture — genuine content-integrity checking of what actually ships.

**A real test-writing mistake, investigated carefully, then resolved with the user directly rather than unilaterally**: T-J4's first draft asserted `action: Log` universally and correctly failed against `assets/jurisdiction/global.json`'s real `Warn`/`RequireConfirmation`/`Block` rules (discrimination, child exploitation/abuse, self-harm — hand-curated from UDHR/CRC articles, predating the Log-only convention). The test-writing fork checked the actual file content and the codebase's own documentation before concluding this looked like a historically intentional exception, not a content bug — a reasonable, appropriately cautious read given it couldn't ask the user directly, and it correctly avoided silently weakening child-safety/self-harm enforcement on its own judgment. But "was intentional when written" and "is still the right call under the now-stricter, store-wide convention" are different questions — this got surfaced to the user explicitly (a real content-safety decision, not a unilateral one), and they chose to **downgrade global.json's 7 rules to Log**, matching every other file and the project's own precedent (a fork earlier this session made the identical fix for eu/gb/ch/no files that had copied global.json's original pattern). global.json's content and the T-J4 test are now both updated: the Log-only check applies to every file, no exemption remains. Added a decision_rule to methodology 29 (below) capturing this exact follow-up nuance for next time.

**A real, small, preventive fix applied while in the area**: `load_jurisdiction_rules` (`src/orchestrator/jurisdiction.rs`) had the identical absolute-path garbage-join bug class ZCode found and fixed in `amt_loop.rs`'s `try_reexpand_one` tonight (`format!("{}/{}", data_dir, object_store_path)` with no `is_absolute()` check). Applied the same defensive fix here too — latent, not yet observed live (jurisdiction container paths are always relative today), but the same bug pattern already bit this codebase once tonight and this call site had the exact same shape.

**Verified, not just claimed**: `cargo test --lib` real exit code 0, 53/53 passed (up from the 39 baseline — includes this block plus whatever landed concurrently), zero failures. `cargo build --release` real exit code 0, zero errors, confirming the two production-code extractions didn't change real boot behavior (same logic, now delegated to tested pure functions).

`docs/GRAPH_TEST_PLAN.md` section 1 fully checked off. File claims (`src/lib.rs`, `src/orchestrator/jurisdiction.rs`, `src/hardware_region.rs`, `src/config/mod.rs`, the test plan doc, this file) released after this write.

**Collaboration feedback**: the mock-vs-live two-layer testing discipline ZCode articulated earlier tonight (deterministic unit tests never call a real model; the live system is where "actually synced" claims get proven) is the right frame and this block followed it — T-J2's real on-disk storage/traversal fixture and T-J4's real shipped-file walk are the "no mocks where the claim is about real behavior" half; T-J1/T-J5's pure-function extraction is the "keep the deterministic layer from rotting silently" half. One thing worth carrying forward: writing the content-honesty test (T-J4) surfaced a real gap in my own working assumption about this codebase's own documented exceptions — worth periodically re-reading a module's own doc comments before writing an integrity test against it, not just its current data shape.

One new real methodology, drawn directly from the T-J4 mistake:

- **29: Verify Test Assumptions Against Documented Exceptions Before Trusting a Failure** — when a new test fails against real shipped data, check whether the codebase's own documentation already describes a legitimate exception before concluding the data is wrong; a test failure is evidence of *a* mismatch, not proof of which side (test or content) is actually wrong. (Numbered 29, not 28 — a sibling fork claimed 28 concurrently; checked the live index before writing the file to avoid a real collision.) **Extended afterward** with a second decision_rule once the global.json resolution above played out: when the documented exception governs real safety/policy content, "was intentional historically" isn't the same question as "is still right now" — surface it to the user rather than resolving unilaterally either direction.

## Task 62 — AMT re-expansion prompt was zero-shot with no guidance; now wired to real methodology + relationship content, real test proves it and caught 2 more real bugs

Direct follow-up to a gap found while answering the user's push on whether AMT re-expansion is "truly guided" (not covered by ZCode's ripple-trigger work or task 58's test blocks, both independently verified separately). `try_reexpand_one` (`src/orchestrator/amt_loop.rs`) built its deepening prompt from only `root_content`/`target_content` — two plain strings — never reading `AMTNode.methodology_ids: Vec<u64>` or `AMTNode.relationships: Vec<AMTRelation>`, both real, already-populated fields on the exact node being deepened. The mechanism to use `methodology_ids` (`load_methodology_rules_text`) already existed and works correctly elsewhere (`stages.rs:1620`) — this was purely a wiring gap in the re-expansion loop specifically.

**Fixed**: `find_unverified_content` (returned only a content string) replaced with `find_unverified_node` (returns the node itself), so `try_reexpand_one` can read its `methodology_ids`/`relationships`. For each methodology id, looks up the container and calls `PromptOrchestrator::load_methodology_rules_text` to inject real decision-rules text. For each relationship, finds the target node (new `find_node_by_id` helper) and surfaces its content as related-branch context. Both injections are additive/best-effort — a methodology id or relationship target that doesn't resolve is silently skipped, not an error, since guidance absence shouldn't block a re-expansion that would otherwise succeed.

**Real test, not just a compile check**: `reexpansion_prompt_includes_methodology_guidance_and_related_branches` extends `MockExecutor` with a `success_capturing` constructor that records the actual prompt text sent, builds a purpose-specific store (the shared `MockStore` can't represent a methodology container at a third id), and asserts the captured prompt genuinely contains both the injected decision-rule text and the related branch's content — not just that the call returned `Ok`.

**Caught 2 real bugs while writing this test** (both mine, both fixed before calling it done):
1. Test fixture typo: used `"RelatedTo"` for `AMTRelationType`, which doesn't have that variant (`AMTRelationType` ≠ the ZSEI-level `RelationType` — the two enums share a naming pattern but not variant names; the real ones are `DependsOn/Requires/RelatesTo/Contradicts/Elaborates/SharedContext`).
2. **A genuine third occurrence of tonight's absolute-path garbage-join bug class** (`amt_loop.rs`'s `try_reexpand_one`, `jurisdiction.rs`'s `load_jurisdiction_rules`, now `amt.rs`'s `load_methodology_rules_text`) — `format!("{}/{}", data_dir, object_store_path)` with no `is_absolute()` check. Every prior real call site to `load_methodology_rules_text` happened to pass a relative path, so this was genuinely latent until a test used an absolute temp-dir fixture path and exposed it. Fixed the same way as the other two. Confirmed via grep that all three occurrences in `src/` are now correctly guarded, no fourth instance found.

**Verified, not just claimed**: `cargo test --lib` real exit 0, 54/54 passed. `cargo build --release` real exit 0, zero errors, binary mtime confirmed newer than every touched file. File claims released.

## global.json reconsidered on restart — Block was real enforcement, downgrading it was a regression, not a relabel. Real Warn/RequireConfirmation enforcement built.

The earlier "downgrade all 7 global.json rules to Log" resolution (previous entries above) got revisited by the user after a restart, and they were right to push on it: consistency-for-its-own-sake isn't worth losing real, working safety enforcement.

**Real investigation, not assumption**: grepped every real comparison against `RuleAction`'s variants in the request path. Found exactly one: `rule.action == RuleAction::Block` triggers a genuine early-return `Err`, stopping the request before any processing — real, working enforcement. `Warn` and `RequireConfirmation` had **zero differentiated behavior** anywhere — both got recorded identically to `Log` at runtime. So the earlier downgrade was two different things wearing one label: a real regression (removing Block's actual teeth from the 2 CRC-cited exploitation/abuse rules) bundled with a cosmetic no-op (Warn/RequireConfirmation never did anything special to begin with).

**Resolved with the user directly, again** (this is real safety-consequential territory, not something to flip back and forth on unilaterally): restored global.json's original 7 actions (Warn×2, RequireConfirmation×3, Block×2), and — per explicit direction ("fix fix all") — built genuinely new, real enforcement for the two previously-inert actions rather than just relabeling:

- **`JurisdictionGateResult`** gained `warnings: Vec<String>` and `confirmations: Vec<(JurisdictionRule, GateResult)>` (reusing the existing `GateResult` type, not inventing a parallel one).
- **`categorize_jurisdiction_matches`** (new, pure, extracted for testability): routes each matched rule by action. Block sets `blocked`; Warn pushes a real, human-readable warning string; RequireConfirmation is collected for review; Log is a no-op. Fully unit-tested (routing across all 4 actions + zero-match case), no mocking needed since it's pure.
- **`resolve_confirmation_reviews`** (new, async, deliberately takes only executor + matches + a few scalars — not the full `OrchestrationState`/store — specifically so it's testable without mocking the entire rule-loading chain): for each RequireConfirmation match, calls the **same decision_gate pipeline (#39) the Consciousness Gate already uses** (`stage_5_consciousness_gate`), framed around the specific matched rule (condition, legal source, truncated prompt) rather than the whole request. A genuine `Decline` sets `blocked = true`; `Proceed` doesn't; a failed pipeline call is recorded honestly as `ReviewFailed` (never a fabricated approval) and fails open. 3 real async tests: Decline blocks, Proceed doesn't, a failed call is visible and non-blocking.
- **A separate, real, connected gap found and fixed along the way**: `OrchestrationState.jurisdiction_gate_result` was **write-only** — set every request, referenced by name in a blocked request's own error message, but never actually mapped into `OrchestrationResponse` the way the sibling `consciousness_gate` field is. So even a real `Block` never let the caller see which rule matched or why. Added `OrchestrationResponse.jurisdiction_gate`, wired into both `build_success_response` and `build_error_response`.
- **T-J4 updated accordingly**: the Log-only assertion now exempts `global.json` by filename specifically (not a general "hand-curated content" heuristic) — a future file wanting the same treatment needs its own explicit round with the user, not just a resemblance to this one.
- **New methodology 30: Enforcement Actions Need Real Behavior, Not Just Labels** — the core lesson (verify what an enforcement enum's variants actually DO in the real code path before making a severity decision on the label alone), plus the write-only-result-field lesson as a second principle.

**Verified, not just claimed, at every step**: `cargo test --lib` real exit 0, 59/59 passed (5 new tests — one of my own test's haystack strings had a real substring-matching bug, "discrimination" doesn't contain "discriminate", caught and fixed before calling it done). `cargo build --release` real exit 0, zero errors, binary mtime fresh. Both `assets/jurisdiction/global.json` and its `target/release/zsei_data/` mirror updated. `bootstrap.rs`'s methodology index updated to 30 (also caught methodology 29's index entry was missing despite its content file already existing — added both 29 and 30 together).

## Task 56 closed: real graph traversal now wired into step context assembly — the original foundational architecture finding from earlier tonight

The very first real architecture gap found tonight (`context_aggregation`'s `ForStep` handler used a flat, one-shot keyword search — `search_containers_by_keywords(&keyword_list, None)` — with `TraversalEngine` never called from this path at all) is now fixed. The type-exclusion fix that shipped earlier tonight stopped the worst symptom (Pipeline-registry hallucination); this closes the deeper ask.

**Built, mirroring the proven pattern from `src/orchestrator/jurisdiction.rs`'s `load_jurisdiction_rules`** (exact-match seed search → real `TraversalMode::Structural` request per seed → merge results): new `traverse_from_seeds(seed_ids)` in `assets/pipelines/general/context_aggregation/main.rs` issues a real `ZSEIQuery::Traverse` over `/zsei/query` for up to `MAX_TRAVERSAL_SEEDS` (4) of the flat keyword search's own hits, `max_depth: 2`, `max_results: 10` per seed — deliberately conservative since this runs on every step of every live request, not an occasional gate check like jurisdiction's. Discovered ids are merged into the existing candidate list before `resolve_containers`/`build_context_from_containers` — the existing `MAX_RESOLVED_CONTAINERS` cap (8) and the infrastructure-type exclusion filter both apply unconditionally to the merged set, untouched, regardless of which path (keyword or traversal) found a given id.

**Caught and fixed one real bug on the way**: `zsei_query`'s raw HTTP response wraps `ZSEIQueryResult` as an externally-tagged enum — `{"TraversalResult": {"containers": [...], ...}}` — not the bare `{"containers": [...]}` I first wrote by analogy by mistake. Caught by checking the actual `ZSEIQueryResult` enum definition and the established unwrapping pattern every other helper in this same file already uses (`fetch_container`'s `"Container"`, `search_containers_by_keywords`'s `"Containers"`) before running anything, not after a failure.

**Why this is more than a mechanical wiring exercise tonight specifically**: `Relation` edges in the general container graph used to be almost entirely empty outside jurisdiction's own content. Tonight, text and code modality (both already built, tested, and live-verified separately) now write real bidirectional `SimilarTo` edges between genuinely related content whenever a graph is created — so this traversal wiring and the relation-edge-writing work are two halves of one real capability, and tonight is the first point both halves actually exist together.

**Live-verified end to end, not just compiled**: built the standalone crate (real exit 0, clean). Created two fresh code-modality containers via the real pipeline binary against the running host — `auth_flow_handler`/`crate::sessions`/`rust` (E) and `other_function_xyz`/`crate::sessions`/`rust` (F) — sharing exactly 2 terms (enough to auto-link, per text/code modality's own ≥2-shared-term threshold) while each keeping one unique term the other doesn't have. Ran `context_aggregation`'s real `ForStep` action three times against the live host: querying E's unique term (`auth_flow_handler`) returned **both** E and F in the sources — F only reachable via the real traversal, not the flat keyword match, since F's keywords don't contain that term at all. Querying F's unique term correctly returned both in the reverse direction too (bidirectional edge, bidirectional discovery). A third, genuinely unrelated query term returned zero sources — confirming the traversal doesn't leak unrelated content or silently match everything.

**Honestly left open**: `ContextSource` doesn't yet carry traversal-vs-keyword provenance (which path found a given source) — the original task's third goal ("what gets passed into a step's context should be a persistent, structured, loggable/queryable object") is already partially served by `StoredTaskStep.context_assembled`/`context_sources` (real, confirmed by reading the actual field definitions in `src/task/mod.rs`, wired by ZCode earlier tonight), but neither currently distinguishes provenance at that level of detail. Not done here — would touch `ContextSource`'s struct shape and its downstream consumers, a real but separate follow-up, not forced into this pass.

## Methodology store audit (fork) — one real, significant pre-existing gap found; 3 new real methodologies added (31-33); quality of the 17-30 tier spot-checked, no fabricated/redundant entries found

**The most significant finding: 12 of the 16 "original bootstrap" methodologies (ids 1, 2, 6-15) have index entries in `src/bootstrap.rs`'s `get_default_methodology_index()` but NO real content file — neither in `assets/methodologies/` nor in the live `target/release/zsei_data/methodologies/`.** Confirmed via direct `ls`: only methods 3, 4, 5, and 16-30 (now 33) physically exist; "Conversational Response" (1), "Contextual Awareness" (2), "Impact-First Development" (6), "Test-Driven Development" (7), "Incremental Development" (8), "Data Analysis Methodology" (9), "API Design Principles" (10), "Documentation Standards" (11), "Technical Writing" (12), "Exploratory Analysis" (13), "Formal Proof Methods" (14), and "Mathematical Rigor" (15) are index-only. Traced the real consequence: `MethodologyStore::register_all` (`src/methodologies/store.rs`) registers a real ZSEI container from index metadata alone — name/categories become keywords/topics — without checking the referenced file exists, so these 12 DO get registered as real, discoverable containers, each pointing its `object_store_path` at a file that was never there. Any real call site that later tries to load one's actual guidance (`load_methodology_rules_text`) fails soft (`.ok()?`, no panic) and silently returns no guidance — so a step could carry a non-empty `methodology_ids` entry that resolves to genuinely nothing. Not fixed in this pass: writing real, substantive content for 12 generic software-engineering topics not grounded in this specific codebase's actual history would risk being exactly the kind of generic/fabricated content this project's own standard (method 21's "skip honestly rather than pad", now echoed in method 33) exists to prevent. Flagging clearly rather than padding — a real, scoped follow-up for whoever wants to write genuine content for these 12, or a decision to retire the phantom entries instead.

**Spot-checked the 17-30 tier for quality issues** (redundant rationale, generic/fabricated content, broken index references) — found none. Every entry's `rationale` ties to a real, specific, dated finding from this actual session; no two are redundant enough to warrant consolidation; all 14 index entries (17-30) have real backing files, confirmed present in both `assets/` and the live data dir.

**3 new real methodologies added, each grounded in a specific real finding not yet captured**, read from `CHECKLIST.md`'s earlier (pre-tonight-session-summary) sections directly rather than guessed:
- **31: Verify What's Actually Active, Not Just What's Built** — from the OpenRouter routing bug (free-tier infra was fully built, but `api_model` still defaulted to the paid `openrouter/auto`, silently masked by a working-but-slow BitNet fallback all night).
- **32: Self-Assessment Prompts Need a Calibration Bar** — from the over-eager `needs_clarification` bug (an unconditional "field non-empty → block" gate on a self-assessment prompt with no calibration guidance made a completely unambiguous "say hello" request trigger 5 clarifying questions).
- **33: Verify the Artifact, Not the Status** — from tonight's own most recent finding (two dispatched forks hit a real mid-run rate limit; one's real, correct work survived under a "failed" status with only its write-up lost; the other's "completed" report included an out-of-scope claim that turned out genuinely true on independent verification). Directly generalizes the exact discipline just practiced.

**Verified, not just claimed**: all 3 new JSON files parse clean (`python3 -m json.tool`). Mirrored into `target/release/zsei_data/methodologies/`, index updated in both `src/bootstrap.rs` and the live `index.json` (methodology_count: 33). `cargo build --release`: real exit code 0 (confirmed via the build log directly, plus binary mtime newer than every `src/` file — `find src/ -newer target/release/ozone-studio` returns nothing). **Honestly still open**: the 12-phantom-methodology gap (above) — not resolved, deliberately not padded. ## Real gap found: text modality's cross-relationship linking has never actually fired through the real orchestrator path — internal keyword/entity/topic extraction silently fails end to end

Tonight's cross-relationship-linking verification (text and code modality) was real, but every test used direct pipeline-binary invocation with hand-crafted `TextAnalysisResult`/`CodeAnalysisResult` fixtures carrying pre-set keywords — it never went through the actual `/orchestrate` request path, which is what a real user interaction uses. Ran that real path for the first time tonight and found a genuine, reproducible break.

**Reproduction**: sent a real `POST /orchestrate` request (task_id 63) with two attached, inline text files sharing meaningful real content (quantum key distribution / BB84 protocol / photon polarization — ≥2 real shared terms). The request succeeded end to end (real model response from `nvidia/nemotron-3-ultra-550b-a55b:free`, jurisdiction gate ran with 99 real rules loaded, blocked=false). But the resulting `ModalityGraph` containers (checked directly on disk: 30330, 30331, 30332) all have **empty keywords, empty topics, empty relationships** — meaning `link_related_containers` never had anything to search for, because the upstream analysis that's supposed to derive keywords/topics from the real text never produced any.

**Root-caused precisely, not guessed**: text modality's keyword/entity/topic extraction (`extract_entities: true, extract_topics: true` in the real `Analyze` action) depends on a real internal LLM call, made by spawning pipeline 9 (`prompt`) as a **nested child subprocess** from inside text modality's own `SubprocessExecutor` (`assets/pipelines/modalities/text/main.rs:6460`). That nested pipeline-9 invocation fails with `"API endpoint not configured"` (confirmed directly: ran pipeline 9's binary standalone, same exact error) — meaning the `OZONE_API_ENDPOINT`/`OZONE_MODEL_TYPE` env vars aren't reaching it. Traced the full chain that's *supposed* to supply them and found no obvious break: `config.toml`'s `[models]` section genuinely has `api_endpoint` set; `config.models.to_pipeline_env()` correctly exports it; `src/lib.rs:242` calls `std::env::set_var` on all of it unconditionally at boot, every boot, no gating; `src/pipeline/executor.rs`'s real subprocess-spawn code (`Command::new(pipeline_path)...`) never calls `.env_clear()` or otherwise touches the environment, so it should inherit the host's full environment by default — same for text modality's own nested spawn of pipeline 9. Every piece of this chain reads as correct in isolation, yet the live, reproducible behavior contradicts it.

**Not fixed — genuinely bigger than a small, confidently-fixable bug, scoping honestly rather than guessing**: the remaining gap is empirical, not conceptual — something in this chain doesn't behave the way the code reads, and nailing the exact point needs real runtime instrumentation (e.g. a temporary debug print of `std::env::var("OZONE_API_ENDPOINT")` immediately before text modality's nested `Command::new(&path)` call, then rebuilding and re-triggering a real request) that a static read alone can't resolve with confidence, and I did not want to guess-and-ship a fix for something this central to whether cross-relationship linking (task 57, claimed done and re-verified via direct binary tests tonight) actually functions for real users.

**Real, concrete implication**: task 57's cross-relationship linking is proven correct in isolation (the linking logic itself, given real keywords, works exactly as designed — independently reconfirmed multiple times tonight) but is **not currently reachable through a real user request** for either text or code modality, since both depend on the same internal-LLM-extraction path through pipeline 9. This is a more significant, more honest status than "done" — recommend re-opening this specifically (not task 57's linking logic, which is fine) as its own tracked item.

Two live test containers from this reproduction (30330, 30331, 30332) left on the host, harmless empty-content synthetic graphs, not cleaned up — consistent with tonight's convention, flagged here for visibility since they don't carry the useful signal other test containers tonight did (no keywords to demonstrate anything with).

## Jurisdiction source registry + maintenance protocol — built per explicit user request

The user asked for a real protocol to verify and maintain jurisdiction content over time, based on real public sources, and pointed out every rule already carries a real `source` citation (plus `official_source`/`retrieved_snippet` fields on `JurisdictionRule`, already defined, already populated on most rules). The ask: aggregate these into a real, usable "approved sources" list, and establish a repeatable maintenance protocol.

**Built, generated from real content, not hand-typed**: `tools/generate_jurisdiction_registry.py` walks every real file under `assets/jurisdiction/` (global.json + all `national/*.json`) and produces `assets/jurisdiction/SOURCE_REGISTRY.md` (human-readable, grouped by scope, one table per country: topic/action/source/official/snippet) and `SOURCE_REGISTRY.json` (machine-readable, same data). Both mirrored into `target/release/zsei_data/jurisdiction/`. Re-run after any jurisdiction content change — regenerated, not maintained by hand, so it can't drift out of sync.

**Real numbers from the first generation**: 147 rules across 41 scopes. Gap report (a genuine cross-check, not just a listing): 76 rules where `official_source` isn't confirmed `true` (not necessarily wrong — many are legitimately hand-curated or method-21-compliant secondary citations, but a real worklist for future verification passes), 53 rules with no `retrieved_snippet` (older content, predates that field being populated), and — a useful sanity check this generation run for free — **0 national-scope rules with a non-Log action**, confirming the Log-only convention genuinely holds across all 40 country files after tonight's several expansion passes.

**New methodology 34 (Jurisdiction Source Maintenance Protocol)**: codifies checking the registry before an independent new source search (consistency with already-approved sources for that country), treating the gap report as a real worklist, and regenerating after every content change. **Deliberately did not add per-rule `verified_at` timestamp tracking to the `JurisdictionRule` schema** — considered it, but judged a real schema change (touching all 41 files, requiring a full build/test cycle) wasn't worth it when the registry's own generation timestamp already gives an honest "aggregated as of" marker at the collection level. Documented as a deliberate, reasoned deferral in the methodology itself, not a silent gap — revisit only if a real, concrete need for per-rule granularity emerges.

**Verified**: registry files are valid (`.md` readable, `.json` parses), gap counts sanity-checked against a direct read of sample files. `cargo build --release` (needed since `src/bootstrap.rs`'s index was updated for methodology 34): real exit code 0, confirmed directly, not via `| tail`. File claims released.

## 2026-09-20 session 4 follow-up (zcode) — jurisdiction edge variance ROOT-CAUSED + fixed

**User-visible symptom**: "Jurisdiction graph: wired N relationship edges" printed a different N every boot (27/35/24) on identical data — never converged to 0, meaning the boot wiring wasn't idempotent in practice.

**Root cause chain (all verified against the live graph)**:
1. Root 7 holds **298 children with only 67 unique names** — 41 scope codes duplicated up to 8× (historical artifacts from boots before the registration dedup existed; registration itself now correctly reports "0 new registrations" every boot).
2. Boot wiring built `scope_to_id` with `HashMap::insert` — with duplicates present, the LAST child_ids match wins = the NEWEST duplicate each boot. Every boot therefore wired edges onto a fresh never-wired scaffold, so the count never converged and edges scattered across duplicates.
3. **Fix**: `entry().or_insert()` — first occurrence in child_ids (insertion order) = the OLDEST container = deterministic canonical target. After one wiring pass on the canonical set, subsequent boots converge to wired=0 (the idempotency T-J5 asserts, now true in practice).

**Proposed cleanup (awaiting user)**: ~231 duplicate empty scaffolds under root 7 (the non-oldest copies per scope). They carry valid object_store_paths to the real rule files but split the graph's jurisdiction identity. Recommend DeleteContainer on the non-oldest duplicates once the canonical-resolution binary is confirmed (next boot should log wired≈41 once, then 0). Not executed — destructive, user decides.

Operator restarted twice (21:33, then 23:53 after a Ctrl+C). Read both boot logs directly rather than assuming anything.

- **Methodology index converged**: first restart showed `Methodology store: 0 new registrations (34 total in index)` — the live `index.json` was still missing entries 17-34 at that point, contradicting ZCode's session-5 claim of having already backfilled them (an already-running host doesn't re-read the file, so this just reflects when the backfill actually landed on disk relative to boot time). Second restart shows `(44 total in index)` — the backfill is now genuinely live and registered. Consistent, no action needed.
- **Jurisdiction duplicates re-checked on the confirmed-current binary**: still 68 total containers / 41 unique scopes / 27 duplicated (unchanged from the pre-restart count). Both boot logs show `Jurisdiction store: 0 new registrations (41 scopes already registered)` and **no** "wired N relationship edges" line at all — meaning the min-id-canonical fix is correctly preventing any *new* duplication (0 new registrations both times), but the 27 leftover duplicates from before are inert cleanup debt, not an active/recurring bug. Same remediation as before: a one-time `DeleteContainer` pass on the 27 non-canonical duplicates (mirroring the earlier 231-container cleanup), whenever the user wants to approve it.
- **Real new bug found live, then fixed and verified**: the second boot log shows the meta-loop getting a real, substantive, non-placeholder draft from BitNet (attempt=4, passed every content gate) — then `MethodologyCreate` hard-failed with `Parse error: missing field 'confidence'`, discarding real work. Root cause: `HeuristicInput.confidence: f32` (`assets/pipelines/general/methodology_create/main.rs`) is a **required field that is never read anywhere downstream** (confirmed by grep — the canonical-shape mapping a few lines below doesn't carry it into the persisted `Heuristic`, which correctly and honestly leaves `examples`/`applicability` empty rather than fabricating them, per the file's own already-existing, well-reasoned comment about DTO/canonical-shape relabeling). A model's real, valid JSON simply omitting an unused field shouldn't discard genuine work. **Fixed**: `confidence: f32` → `#[serde(default)] confidence: Option<f32>` (same honest-absence idiom as `GateResult.confidence` elsewhere in this codebase — never fabricates a value, just accepts its absence). Built clean (real exit 0). **Live-verified directly**: fed the pipeline binary the exact previously-failing shape (a heuristic with no `confidence` key at all) against the running host — real success, `methodology_id: 30423` created. Real test container 30423 left on the host (harmless synthetic content, not cleaned up, matching this session's established convention).
- This means the meta-loop's automated methodology-creation path may have **never actually succeeded end-to-end before now** whenever a model's draft omitted this unused field — worth watching the next real AMT/meta-loop pass to confirm a draft now persists successfully instead of failing at the create step.

## 2026-09-21 (claude-code) — real "living graph" integration test across all 3 built modalities; found + fixed a real bug blocking every math-modality attachment

Per the user's direction: set up a genuine end-to-end test, not a synthetic one. First confirmed which modalities are actually buildable/testable — only text (100), code (101), math (105) have a real built pipeline binary; the other ~22 are source-only (never built), so "test all modalities" honestly means these three, not a broader claim.

Created 3 real files sharing genuine cross-referencing content (gradient descent: the concept in `.txt`, a real implementation in `.rs`, the real derivation in `.tex`) in a scratch dir, and sent a real `POST /orchestrate` request against project 3 (the established test workspace, confirmed from prior session notes) with all three attached.

**Real bug found on the very first attempt — the whole request failed**: `"Failed to parse input: unknown variant \`Analyze\`, expected one of \`ParseExpression\`, \`AnalyzeProof\`, ...\`"` for pipeline_id 105 (math). Root cause, confirmed by reading the code directly: `process_modality` (`src/orchestrator/graphs.rs`) sends every attached file a generic `"Analyze"` action — this already had a real, honest doc comment acknowledging it only really works for text/code and defaults everything else to a best guess. But **math modality was built since that comment was written** (this session, even) and was never given its own real branch — and math's real `MathAction` enum has **no `Analyze` variant at all** (it's `ParseExpression`/`AnalyzeProof`/`VerifyStep`/etc., a genuinely different action vocabulary, not just a differently-named field within the same shape). So any `.tex`/`.latex`/`.nb` attachment failed outright, and — a real, secondary finding worth noting — that one file's failure aborted the *entire* orchestration request rather than degrading gracefully to the text+code attachments that would have otherwise worked fine.

**Fixed**: added a real branch for `pipeline_id == 105` sending `ParseExpression{expression, format: "LaTeX", extract_variables: true, simplify: false}` — the real, closest equivalent to "analyze this raw content" in math's actual action set. Built clean (real exit 0). **This is a host-binary change (`src/orchestrator/graphs.rs`), not a pipeline-crate change — needs a restart to take effect**, unlike the pipeline-only fixes earlier tonight. Re-running the same 3-file living-graph test after the next restart to confirm text+code+math all produce real graphs with real cross-relationship edges between them.

## 2026-09-21/22 (claude-code) — living-graph test re-run: found a SECOND real math bug (masked by the first), fixed + verified live across two independent runs; text/code cross-relationships double-confirmed on disk

Re-ran the exact same request after the `Analyze`→`ParseExpression` fix landed. It came back `success: true`, but I didn't stop there — independently checked the response's own stage summary (`"Initial Graph Creation" → "1 modality graphs created (text), cross-modal: 0"`) and the actual on-disk containers rather than trusting the top-level success flag alone. This surfaced a second, genuinely distinct bug that the first fix had been masking.

**Bug 2 — `analysis`/`analysis_result` field-name + shape mismatch, in TWO call sites.** Both `src/orchestrator/mod.rs` (STEP 0, the per-attached-file loop in stage 2 — the one that actually ran) and `src/orchestrator/graphs.rs::create_initial_modality_graphs` (stage 4b, currently dormant for this test — see gap below) build a generic `CreateGraph` action with `"analysis_result": analysis.get("analysis")...` for every modality's graph-persist call. That's correct for text/code, but math's real `MathAction::CreateGraph` requires a field literally named `analysis`, typed as a specific `MathAnalysisResult{analysis_type, parse_result, proof_analysis, confidence}` struct — not an arbitrary re-keyed blob. Sending the wrong shape failed with `"missing field \`analysis\`"` on every math attachment, real work silently discarded (`.unwrap_or_default()` swallowed the error and minted a fresh orphan `graph_id` that was never actually persisted). A **third**, related issue compounded this: math's `CreateGraph` result nests `graph_id` under `result` (`MathResult` is `#[serde(untagged)]`, no top-level convenience field), unlike code's `CodeModalityOutput` which puts `graph_id` at the top level — so even a successful math `CreateGraph` call would have had its id silently lost by the shared top-level-only extraction. **Fixed** both call sites: math now gets its own branch constructing a real `MathAnalysisResult` (wrapping `ParseExpression`'s own `parse_result` output, confidence carried through) and extracting `graph_id` from the correct nested path. Built clean (real exit 0, 13m48s).

**Re-tested live after restart (binary 20:02:26, boot 20:07:15 — confirmed live before testing).** Real, independently-verified results, not taken from the response alone:
- Math now gets a **real persisted `ModalityGraph` container** (id 30433, "Expression math graph (20 nodes, 19 edges, 0 proof steps)") instead of silently failing — bug 2 confirmed fixed.
- **Text↔code cross-relationships independently reconfirmed on disk across two separate full runs** (containers 30425/30426 from the first run, 30427/30428/30431/30432 from the second) — real `SimilarTo` edges, real varying confidence (0.9 exact-pair / 0.75 / 0.45 partial-overlap), `discovered_via: TextAnalysis`/`CodeAnalysis`. This is the living-graph proof the user asked for, for 2 of 3 modalities, now shown twice.
- **New, honest finding — math's graph is real but isolated (`relationships: []`)**: checked the persisted graph content directly (`zsei_data/graphs/math_464507284.json`) and found the root cause — `ParseExpression` is built to parse one clean mathematical expression, not a whole prose document. Fed the full `.tex` file (mostly English paragraphs plus some LaTeX), it character-tokenized the prose and extracted spurious single-letter "variables" (`d`, `o`, `c`, `u`, `m`, `e`, `n`, `t`, ... — literally the distinct letters appearing anywhere in the text, each typed `Unknown`). `derive_math_keywords` then produced a single-letter keyword set with zero meaningful overlap against the text/code graphs' real keywords (`gradient`, `theta`, `learning_rate`, `epsilon`, `convergence`), so `link_related_containers`'s ≥2-shared-term threshold correctly found nothing to link. Math genuinely has no content-ingestion action equivalent to text's entity/topic extraction for mixed prose+math documents — this is an architecture gap, not a crash, and not fixed in this pass. Flagged for the user/ZCode to decide: worth a dedicated fix (e.g. deriving math's file-graph keywords from real text-style extraction over the raw content as a supplement to `ParseExpression`'s per-token variables) versus accepting it as a known limitation for now.

**Still-open, separate, pre-existing gap (not touched this pass):** stage 4b (`create_initial_modality_graphs`, also fixed for the same bug 2 pattern for when it's reached) never actually runs for code/math in this test — `classified_file_graphs` comes back empty because the file-role-classification LLM call's `"response"` field is coming back genuinely empty (`raw=""`, `EOF while parsing a value`) despite a real non-zero `tokens_used`, so `root_modality_list.verified_modalities` only ever contains `"text"`. This is why that stage's own summary line still reads `"1 modality graphs created (text), cross-modal: 0"` even on the fixed binary — it's a real, different bug (something is producing/extracting an empty completion for this specific call shape) that doesn't block the STEP 0 mechanism which is what actually produces the verified cross-relationships above. Not investigated further this pass; worth a dedicated look.

## 2026-09-22 (claude-code) — MAJOR FINDING, independently verified: `decision_gate` (pipeline 39) is not a real model call — jurisdiction `RequireConfirmation` currently cannot ever decline

Per the user's direction to keep expanding the zero-shot call registry (categorize all calls, quality-review each one, track jurisdiction calls specifically, find missing categories) — dispatched a third fork to expand `docs/ZERO_SHOT_CALL_REGISTRY.md`. It surfaced something serious enough that I independently re-verified every claim myself, directly against source, before accepting it (same discipline as every other finding this session — never trust a fork's report alone).

**Confirmed directly, by reading `assets/pipelines/consciousness/decision_gate/main.rs` (528 lines) myself:**
- Zero `reqwest`/HTTP references anywhere in the file — it never calls pipeline 9 or any model backend. It is a fully deterministic simulation, not a zero-shot call, despite being documented as one.
- `run_ethical_assessment(task_summary, config)` takes `task_summary` as a parameter but **never reads it** — returns 3 hardcoded principle scores (0.95/0.90/0.92, avg ≈ 0.923) with canned reasoning strings, regardless of actual task content.
- `run_emotional_response`/`run_experience_retrieval`/`run_identity_alignment` all take `_task_summary` (underscore-prefixed, compiler-confirmed unused) and return fixed/empty data — `experience.warnings` and `ethical.concerns` are always `Vec::new()`.
- `GateConfig::default()`: `ethical_threshold: 0.7`. Since the hardcoded ethical score (~0.923) always exceeds this, and `concerns`/`warnings` are always empty (so the critical-concern and experience-warning branches in `make_decision` can never trigger), **`make_decision` cannot structurally return `Decline` under default config, for any input.**
- `src/orchestrator/jurisdiction.rs::resolve_confirmation_reviews` (line 367) — confirmed it never passes a config override, only `{action, task_id, task_summary, blueprint_id, user_id}`. `task_summary` is real and content-specific (rule condition + legal source + first 400 chars of the prompt) — genuinely good input, silently discarded by the receiving pipeline. `decision == "Decline"` is the only thing that sets `result.blocked = true`; everything else (including a malformed/missing field, via `.unwrap_or("Proceed")`) defaults to not blocking. A call error is explicitly documented to fail open too (`jurisdiction.rs:391-395`).

**Net effect**: every `RequireConfirmation`-matched jurisdiction rule currently behaves as a no-op — it can never actually block a request, under any real input, with the default config in use. This directly concerns the "restore and fix, build genuinely working enforcement" work done earlier this session (2026-09-15/16, jurisdiction Warn/RequireConfirmation) — that work correctly built the *plumbing* to `decision_gate` and correctly interprets its response, but the underlying decision-maker it plumbs into has always been a hardcoded stub. **This was not broken by anything done this session — it's a pre-existing gap, now found and precisely root-caused for the first time.**

**Also found, directly relevant**: `jurisdiction.rs`'s own doc comment (line ~358-360, and `JurisdictionGateResult.confirmations`'s doc comment) explicitly calls this "a real model-based review, not a fabricated approval." That claim is inaccurate as the code stands — confirmed false by direct read, not a stale-doc guess. Since `decision_gate` (pipeline 39) is shared, per that same comment, with the Consciousness Gate, this same gap likely affects consciousness-gate decisions too, not jurisdiction alone — not independently re-verified for the consciousness-gate call site specifically this pass, worth confirming.

**Not fixed — real decision needed, not a mechanical fix**: either (a) wire `decision_gate`'s `Evaluate` arm to a genuine pipeline-9 call using `task_summary` (the parameter already exists, just unused), or (b) knowingly keep it as a placeholder simulation and correct the misleading doc comments (jurisdiction.rs, LIVING_GRAPH_STATUS.md, CHECKLIST.md's own prior "real enforcement" language) to stop overstating what it does. This pass was scoped as documentation/review only per explicit user direction ("we are just reviewing... not degradation") — no code changed. Full writeup: `docs/ZERO_SHOT_CALL_REGISTRY.md` §7.

Rest of this fork's work (also in the doc, not independently re-verified line-by-line beyond spot checks): 7 functional categories derived from real call behavior; a correction to the prior fork's row #14 (was mislabeled "jurisdiction compliance," actually a methodology decision-rule check, no jurisdiction involvement — verified); a quality-review pass flagging `amt.rs:2671`'s `max_tokens: 400` by analogy to the already-confirmed truncation bug at `stages.rs:948`; 4 verified (not speculative) missing-category findings: consciousness/ethical decisions (confirmed fully deterministic per above), jurisdiction rule staleness checking (zero coverage), coordination-graph/multi-agent handoff path (zero coverage), code-modality output review beyond tree-sitter structure (zero coverage — plausibly why the earlier-found `function_calls` extraction gap has nothing catching it).

## 2026-09-22 (claude-code) — token-budget audit: 16 of 18 real `max_tokens` call sites are flat hardcoded literals, disconnected from the real per-model dynamic mechanism

User question, precisely targeted: why are `max_tokens` values (400, 800, etc.) fixed absolutes instead of scaling with whatever model is actually configured, and are these secretly tuned against BitNet specifically rather than being model-agnostic. Investigated directly (no fork — fast, bounded grep+read task).

**Finding: the user's premise is correct.** A real, correctly-built, genuinely model-agnostic mechanism already exists — `state.model_context_limit` (`mod.rs:1511-1521`), resolved per-request from `model_config.context_length` → `available_models` → `config.models.context_length` (whatever's actually configured), never a hardcoded per-family guess. There's even a documented, already-fixed prior bug for exactly this failure class (`mod.rs:1366-1375`): `model_context_limit` used to fall back to a hardcoded 200,000-token assumption regardless of configured backend, so a BitNet server (real context 4096) computed `200000/4=50000` and sent `llama-cli -n 50000` — a trivial prompt looked hung for minutes. Fixed.

**But that fix only reached 2 of 18 real `max_tokens` call sites** (`stages.rs:1603`/`1688` — the latter is the highest-volume call in the system, the primary step-execution dispatch, 18-25k tokens/request live). Grepped every `"max_tokens"` occurrence in `src/orchestrator/*.rs` and cross-referenced against the zero-shot registry's §1 table: **16 of 18 are flat literals (150-1000) with zero relationship to `model_context_limit`.**

**Confirmed exactly the failure mode the user described, from the codebase's own history, not inferred**: `stages.rs:943` (zero-shot simulation) is the same call CHECKLIST.md's 2026-09-20 BitNet coherency sweep documents as having genuinely truncated a schema-conforming response at `max_tokens: 400` — "fixed" by bumping to the current flat `800`. That fix landed two call sites away from `stages.rs:1688`, which solves the identical problem correctly by deriving from `model_context_limit`. 400→800 roughly tracks what `model_context_limit/4-8` would already give for BitNet's real ~4096 context specifically — the numbers read as tuned against BitNet's observed behavior, not computed from whatever's actually configured, exactly matching the user's own framing ("not hacking for BitNet when BitNet is just one of many local models"). The same flat values would under-use a large-context cloud model (capping real output far below available budget) and could still truncate on a smaller-than-BitNet local model — the same failure class recurring at a different model size, with nothing in the code to prevent it.

**Per explicit user direction, this pass is capture/audit only — no code changed.** Full table (every site, current value, dynamic-or-not) written into `docs/ZERO_SHOT_CALL_REGISTRY.md` §9, including the real fix precedent to copy when undertaken (`stages.rs:1688`'s pattern, plus `mod.rs:1901`'s existing `.max(256)` floor idiom as a reusable safeguard against a tiny local model's fraction becoming too small for even a short JSON verdict).

## 2026-09-22 (claude-code) — MAJOR CORRECTION: the "empty-response-despite-real-tokens" bug is systemic, not one call site. Only 1 of ~20 real zero-shot call sites actually guards against it.

User caught this live: sent a fresh boot log showing the exact `graphs.rs:491` failure recurring (`tokens_used=Some(965), raw=""`) on a run that used a real, directly-exported `OPENROUTER_API_KEY` (confirmed not a BitNet fallback — that stage finished in ~7s, no fallback WARN logged), and pushed back hard on the framework: a zero-shot call that returns real token spend but unusable content should never be silently accepted — it should retry through zero-shot again, not degrade to empty. Investigated the real root cause and the real scope, and found something more serious than the single-site gap already on record.

**Root cause of the specific empty-response symptom, confirmed by reading the code**: `assets/pipelines/general/prompt/main.rs`'s `call_openai_api` (line 427-430) and `call_anthropic_api` (line ~343-346) each extract response text and token count as two **independent** operations on the same API response: `content = result["choices"][0]["message"]["content"].as_str().unwrap_or("").to_string()` silently defaults to an empty string on ANY shape mismatch (null content, a refusal field, a reasoning-only response, content as an array instead of a string — all real behaviors on OpenRouter's free tier), while `tokens = result["usage"]["total_tokens"].as_u64()...` succeeds independently regardless. This is exactly why `tokens_used=965, response=""` happens — not model noise, not a BitNet quirk (confirmed: this specific recurrence used a real OpenRouter key), a real extraction gap present in BOTH wire-protocol handlers.

**The scope correction**: re-checked exactly which call sites actually call `is_unusable_pipeline9_result` (the function that treats "Ok but empty" the same as a hard error — confirmed exact logic: `response.trim().is_empty()` → `true`) before accepting a pipeline-9 result. Only **one** real call site in the entire codebase does: the primary step-execution dispatch (`stages.rs:1737-1767` — the highest-volume call, answering every actual user request). Its own comment already documents this exact failure mode explicitly: *"Confirmed live this session (repeatedly, via the methodology meta-loop): pipeline 9 (especially OpenRouter) can return `Ok` with a genuinely empty `response` field — a real, recurring backend behavior, not a hard error."* That fix was made once, correctly, with a real retry loop + fallback escalation — and never propagated anywhere else.

**This corrects both my own and ZCode's earlier characterization** of `docs/ZERO_SHOT_CALL_REGISTRY.md`'s §1 table: `stages.rs:356` (#10, Blueprint Assignment) and `stages.rs:948` (#11, Zero-Shot Simulation) are labeled "Yes" for fallback-chain-wired — technically true (they do call `try_fallback_chain`) but materially misleading for this exact bug, since both are gated on `Err(e) => try_fallback_chain(...)` only, with **no `is_unusable_pipeline9_result` check at all** — they would silently accept an empty-but-`Ok` response exactly like `graphs.rs:491` does. Neither ZCode's independent verification nor mine caught this distinction on the first pass — both of us confirmed the "Yes" label was present without checking what specifically it protected against. **Net scope: ~19 of ~20 real zero-shot call sites in this codebase are structurally exposed to silently accepting real-token-spend-but-empty-content results, with only one correctly-guarded reference implementation to copy.**

**Not fixed yet — flagged to the user given the severity, awaiting direction on fix-now vs. continue-documenting** (an explicit "document only, fix later" answer was already given minutes earlier for the adjacent max_tokens question, so scope/timing needs re-confirming given this is a correctness bug degrading live production results, not a sizing inefficiency). The real fix, when undertaken, has an exact, already-proven pattern to copy verbatim from `stages.rs:1737-1767`: wrap each call site's result in an `is_unusable_pipeline9_result` check, retry a bounded number of times, then walk the fallback chain — exactly the "rerun it through zero-shot again, never return empty" principle the user described, already built and working in one place.

## 2026-09-22 (claude-code) — the empty-response bug fixed at all 16 remaining call sites, independently verified, build clean, live on the current binary

User chose "fix all ~19 sites now" after seeing the severity. Dispatched a fork to implement, then independently verified before trusting it (same discipline as every other piece of work this session — reviewed the actual diff at 7+ sites directly, did not just read the fork's self-report).

**Root cause fixed** (`assets/pipelines/general/prompt/main.rs`): `call_anthropic_api` had a real bug, not just a diagnostic gap — it blindly indexed `content[0]["text"]`, which would silently read as empty if a non-text block (e.g. a `thinking` block under extended-thinking configs) ever landed first. Fixed to search all content blocks for the first `type=="text"`. Both `call_anthropic_api` and `call_openai_api` also gained a stderr diagnostic (finish_reason + truncated raw message JSON) that fires only when content ends up empty — never changes behavior, exists purely so a future empty-response case is immediately diagnosable instead of requiring a fresh investigation.

**New shared helper**: `metered_execute_resilient` (`src/orchestrator/mod.rs:2305-2325`) — the exact proven pattern from the one correctly-guarded site (`stages.rs:1727-1770`) extracted into one reusable method: retry loop with backoff using `is_unusable_pipeline9_result`, then `try_fallback_chain` if still unusable.

**16 call sites wired**, independently spot-checked at 7 of them by reading the actual diff (not the fork's description): `graphs.rs:429` (the exact site with tonight's two live failures — verified using `metered_execute_resilient` now), `amt.rs:917/1223/1383/1567/1846/2621/2671` (verified 2621's diff directly), `response.rs:308`, `stages.rs:356/950` (verified both — cleanly replaced the old Err-only `match` with `metered_execute_resilient`, confirmed comments correctly explain the upgrade), `stages.rs:1369/1610/1864`, `mod.rs:2544` (`on_step_complete`).

**One honest partial fix, verified accurate**: `confirm_yes_no_orch` (`mod.rs:~2389`) — its closure has no `OrchestrationState` access (must stay re-callable across the strength-N confirmation loop, structurally incompatible with a `&mut` borrow), so the full fallback-chain walk isn't reachable there without a larger refactor. Got a same-call retry instead (no `state` needed) — read the actual code, confirmed this is a real structural constraint, not corner-cutting, and the fork was upfront about the narrower scope rather than either skipping it or overclaiming.

**Left untouched, confirmed correct**: `amt_loop.rs`/`meta_loop.rs` (the two background loops) — verified directly before dispatching the fork that they already have their own correct mechanism (a real balanced-brace-scan `extract_json_object`, explicit empty-response check, cross-pass model escalation) and didn't need this pattern.

**Verification, independent, not taken from the fork's report**: rebuilt the whole workspace myself (`cargo build --release`, real exit 0, 13 pre-existing warnings only, none new) — the fork's own claimed exit codes (prompt crate 0, workspace 0 after 12m47s) were re-confirmed by a fresh build, not trusted alone. `docs/ZERO_SHOT_CALL_REGISTRY.md` §10 updated with the correction to the #10/#11 "Yes" mislabeling and the full root-cause writeup.

**Live-verification status**: host restarted onto the new binary (built 21:59:26, boot confirmed 02:01:05) — not yet re-exercised against a real orchestration request hitting `graphs.rs:429` specifically to confirm the live fix in practice (the exact call that failed twice tonight). Worth a follow-up real test the next time an attached-file request runs.

## 2026-09-22 (claude-code) — two more forks: does living-graph relationship data reach downstream stages, and does jurisdiction/consciousness context reach AMT/blueprint — plus a real correction to LIVING_GRAPH_STATUS.md's TraversalEngine gap

Continuing the user's direction ("identify more callsites... now with the context and cross-relationships... ensure everything is wired in, text gives the place to all and all needs a place"). Dispatched two parallel forks; independently verified their load-bearing claims directly against source before writing anything up (same discipline throughout).

**Fork 1 — does `state.file_graphs`' real relationship data (the `SimilarTo` edges this session proved get created among a request's own attached files) reach anything downstream, or is it write-only per request?**
Verified every claimed read site directly: `amt.rs:2128`, `graphs.rs:447`, `stages.rs:33` — confirmed none of them carry relationship data, only `path/modality/role/graph_id`. **Confirmed real gap: neither AMT building nor blueprint assignment — the two stages that decide what work to do — ever see the real relationship edges among a request's own files.** They know the files exist and their role; they don't know "file A is 0.9-confident related to file B."

**One real exception found, and independently traced to full confirmation, not left as a flagged discrepancy**: `context_aggregation` (stages 9-11, assembles what an executing step actually sees) genuinely walks real relationship edges. Traced the full call chain myself: `traverse_from_seeds` → `ZSEIQuery::Traverse` → `src/zsei/query.rs:129-131` → `traversal.traverse(...)` → `TraversalMode::Structural` → the literal `TraversalEngine::structural_traversal` (`src/zsei/traversal.rs:48/154`). **This directly contradicts `docs/LIVING_GRAPH_STATUS.md` gap #1's "zero call sites" claim — confirmed stale, not a misreading.** Corrected that doc in place (additive, per its own convention — the original claim kept for history, marked stale, real current state written in). Even this real mechanism is keyword-seeded (not `state.file_graphs`-direct) — probabilistic rediscovery, not a guaranteed link to the exact containers a request just created.

Also confirmed real: `context_aggregation`'s own `context_sources` provenance genuinely distinguishes `"keyword-scan"` vs `"traversal"` labels (two separate code sites, not a leftover stale tag) — meaning `LIVING_GRAPH_STATUS.md` gap #2's own prediction ("provenance becomes `traversal:<mode>` when gap 1 closes") has come true in substance, just without the `:<mode>` suffix originally predicted. Corrected that row too.

**Recommended cheapest-correct fix (not implemented, investigation only)**: AMT building and blueprint assignment already know `state.file_graphs`'s exact graph_ids — no search needed. A direct per-id container fetch reading each one's own `relationships` array, threaded into those prompts as a short summary, closes the most consequential gap far more cheaply than wiring full traversal into two more stages.

**Fork 2 — does jurisdiction/consciousness context actually reach AMT/blueprint/simulation, or are they thin disconnected checkpoints?**
Verified the real stage order directly (`stages.rs:9-97`): Jurisdiction Gate(0) → Build AMT(5) → Blueprint Assignment(6) → Zero-Shot Simulation(7) → Consciousness Gate(8). This ordering matters and was confirmed, not assumed.

**Confirmed already fine**: `jurisdiction_desc` (`stages.rs:757-790`, verified directly) is genuinely rich — real matched rule condition/action/source for up to 5 rules, not a placeholder.

**Confirmed real gap #1 (cheap, verified)**: `jurisdiction_desc` never surfaces `state.jurisdiction_gate_result`'s `warnings` or `confirmations` fields — only the match summary. Pure string-building fix, no new call.

**Confirmed real gap #2 (cheap, verified by direct grep)**: jurisdiction's fully-resolved outcome never reaches AMT building or blueprint assignment — grepped `jurisdiction_gate_result`/`jurisdiction_desc` across `amt.rs`/`stages.rs` myself: the only hits outside jurisdiction.rs are `stages.rs:757`/`:920` (the simulation prompt) and one unrelated `amt.rs:2861` struct-default initialization. All three functions already receive `state: &mut OrchestrationState` — the data is trivially reachable, just never read. This is exactly the "run through blueprint and AMT" gap the user named, precisely scoped now.

**Consciousness — confirmed structurally different, not neglected**: its real decision doesn't exist until stage 8, strictly after simulation (stage 7) — the thin placeholder is the maximum real signal available that early, not a gap of the same kind as jurisdiction's. `consciousness_enabled` does separately reach step-execution context (`stages.rs:1471`).

**Net across both forks + verification**: 3 confirmed, cheap, mechanically-reachable gaps (jurisdiction→AMT/blueprint, jurisdiction_desc's missing fields, file_graphs relationships→AMT/blueprint), 1 real doc correction (TraversalEngine no longer dead code), 1 architectural proposal flagged not recommended (a second AMT-aware jurisdiction pass for Warn/RequireConfirmation tiers). Full writeups: `docs/ZERO_SHOT_CALL_REGISTRY.md` §11-12, `docs/LIVING_GRAPH_STATUS.md` gaps #1-2 corrected in place. None of these implemented this pass — awaiting direction, same as the other open decisions this session.

## 2026-09-22 (zcode) — raw-response forensics → multi-candidate commit logic landed (81/81, binary 09:56)

The orchestrate-7 failure trio ("decision field missing" ×3) got root-caused by direct BitNet call with the exact decision prompt. RAW RESPONSE captured in full: BitNet emits **multiple conflicting JSON candidates** in one response (Decide 0.9 → Reject 0.8 → Proceed 0.7 → Fail 0.6 → Accept 0.5, confidence descending, interspersed with prose, bare `{}`, code fences, and a hallucinated "Your Task:" follow-up). The shared extractor picked the FIRST parseable object = the leading bare `{}` → "decision field missing" on every review.

**Fixes (binary 09:56):**
- `extract_json_object_shared` skips EMPTY parseable objects (a bare `{}` carries nothing — accepting it made every field lookup fail).
- `extract_all_json_objects` (decision_review.rs): returns ALL non-empty parseable candidates in order; the review **commits to the first candidate that normalizes to a valid decision AND carries real reasoning** — the model's first committed judgment, before degenerate re-sampling degrades (confidence descends 0.9→0.4 in the observed output).
- Synonym normalization landed (approve/allow/continue/pass→proceed; deny/reject/block/declined→decline); ambiguous values ("decide") deliberately never map — a review that won't commit is not a review.

**Also verified this pass**: GAP-C1 enrichment is LIVE-VERIFIED on disk — container 1099 (EU ruleset) now carries real content keywords: [personal, data, breach, child, minor, illegal, content, unfair, commercial, practice, artificial]. Content↔jurisdiction edge-mixing can now fire.

**Restart needed** (binary 09:56). Then re-run the RC-matching prompt: expect a REAL Proceed/Decline with cited reasoning in decision_review.jsonl instead of review-failed.
