## 2026-09-22 (claude-code) — full "silent truncation/dropping" audit across the codebase, 7 parallel forks, all independently verified: 4 real bugs found and fixed, the meta-loop "shell discarding" complaint likely explained, one major systemic finding

User's design critique of ZCode's confetti fix (validated, acted on): parsing around bad model output is treating a model-quality problem as a parsing problem — the right fix is detect-and-retry, matching how every other unusable-response case in this codebase is already handled. Also directed: audit EVERY parsing site for the same class of issue (not just confetti — any place a response parses successfully while silently dropping real content), and extend real per-call metrics broadly. Dispatched 7 parallel forks; independently verified every load-bearing claim myself against source before accepting it (same discipline throughout).

**1. Confetti redesigned as retry, not parse-around** (`decision_review.rs`): new `is_confetti()` (>1 non-empty JSON candidate = confetti), wired into the SAME retry-then-fallback trigger as `pipeline9_unusable` (verified directly: `pipeline9_unusable(&result) || is_confetti(...)` at both the retry-loop and fallback-trigger sites). This closes the residual risk found in the earlier parse-around fix as a structural consequence, not a separate patch — confirmed by direct read, single-candidate path unchanged.

**2. Real per-call metrics extended to all 15 real `metered_execute_resilient` call sites** (`mod.rs`): new `capture_zero_shot_call` writes `{data_dir}/model_calls/zero_shot_calls.jsonl` (ts/call_site/model_used/tokens_used/retry_count/used_fallback/success/response_preview) — verified all 15 real call sites directly by grep, each with a distinct real label (`file_role_classification`, 7 AMT-family labels, `response_graph_render`, `blueprint_assignment`, `zero_shot_simulation`, `web_search_decompose`, `context_compaction`, `methodology_compliance_check`, `on_step_complete_alignment`). This is a real, durable, per-model-per-call-site dataset that didn't exist an hour ago — decision_review.jsonl (S10) proved the pattern; this (S11) widens it to everything.

**3. The SAME confetti vulnerability found and fixed in `amt_loop.rs` and `meta_loop.rs`'s own separate extractors** — both had their own independent `extract_json_object` copies (confirmed: 4+ independent extractor implementations exist in this codebase, not 1 shared one, a real architectural finding in itself). **Directly relevant to this session's standing "meta-loop discarding drafts as shells" complaint**: `meta_loop.rs`'s bug meant a leading empty `{}` in a confetti burst would parse fine, `has_decision_rules`/`has_heuristics` would both silently read false, and a genuinely real BitNet draft later in the same response would get logged and discarded as "no real decision_rules or heuristics" — indistinguishable from BitNet actually producing a shell. **This directly challenges the "BitNet is just weaker, needs more loops" framing raised earlier — the capability may already have been there, discarded by a mechanical bug, not a model limitation.** Fixed in both files the same way as `decision_review.rs`: detect >1 candidate, route through each file's own existing retry mechanism (amt_loop's cross-pass `attempts_before` escalation, meta_loop's cycle-retry `continue`). Verified directly — both real, both correctly wired.

**4. Real completeness bug found and fixed in File Role Classification** (`graphs.rs`): the parsed classification array was never checked against `state.file_graphs`' real size — a model returning fewer classifications than files attached, or individual malformed entries silently dropped by `filter_map`, produced a silently-incomplete result with no warning and no retry (distinct from the already-fixed empty-response case). Fixed: a bounded completeness-retry loop that compares classified paths against the real known file set, logs exactly what's missing, retries once, and proceeds honestly with the real partial result if still incomplete after retries. Verified directly.

**5. MAJOR — the long-standing, previously-never-fixed zero-shot-simulation schema-drift bug is now closed** (`stages.rs`, `stage_4_zero_shot_simulation`). This is the exact gap flagged repeatedly across this entire multi-day session (CHECKLIST.md's own BitNet coherency sweep documented it days ago: "valid-JSON-wrong-schema, step_N keys vs step_predictions") and never actually fixed until now. Root cause confirmed: the array-only parse silently produced an empty `Vec` whenever BitNet returned real, substantive prediction data under `step_0`/`step_1`/... keys instead of the requested array — a non-empty, "successful" response that passed every existing check while quietly losing all its content. Fixed with real schema-flexible parsing: tries the array shape first, falls back to scanning `step_N` keys (handling both structured and plain-string drifted values), sorts by step index, logs when the recovery path fires. Verified directly by reading the full implementation — genuinely well-built, not a token fix.

**6. Real bug found and fixed in `methodology_synthesis`** (`amt.rs:2697`): was using raw `find('{')`/`rfind('}')` directly on the whole response (not this file's own existing, better extractor) — a confetti-style response could splice unrelated prose into the span or fail outright, `unwrap_or_default()` silently turning a real draft into nothing. Fixed to use the file's own existing `extract_json_from_response` for consistency.

**7. Real systemic finding, not fixed (correctly out of scope for a single-directive fork), flagged clearly**: `Self::extract_json_from_response` (the naive first-to-last-brace extractor) is used pervasively across `amt.rs`/`stages.rs` beyond the two sites just fixed — every one of those call sites theoretically shares the same confetti-class exposure now closed elsewhere. A real, separate, bigger decision: upgrade the shared extractor itself (one fix, many sites) versus auditing each call site individually. Also flagged, correctly not touched: `stage_3_blueprint_assignment`'s whole-array-drops-on-one-bad-step parsing — real but already-mitigated by an existing designed fallback (empty-then-rebuild), a partial-salvage parse would be a real future improvement, not an active silent-loss bug.

**Docs updated to capture this cycle** (2 dedicated forks, both independently cross-verified real work before writing, caught and fixed additional pre-existing staleness while there): `docs/ZERO_SHOT_CALL_REGISTRY.md` §13 (new — the full extractor-implementation inventory and the "which call sites are protected against what" answer), `docs/CONTEXT_REGISTRY.md` (S11 source added, carrier #4 widened, §4/§5 corrected — including catching that the token-budget-fix status was STILL stale in 2 more places nobody had caught yet), `docs/TOP_DOWN_REVIEW_GUIDE.md` §1/§3.6 (doctrine connection to S11, confetti finding rewritten to describe the real retry-based redesign).

**Independently verified by me directly, not taken from any fork's word**: `is_confetti`'s detection logic and its use in the retry trigger; all 15 real `metered_execute_resilient` call sites and their labels; the File Role Classification completeness-retry logic; `parse_json_object`'s naive implementation and its use at both `stages.rs:506` and `stages.rs:1068`; both `amt_loop.rs`/`meta_loop.rs` confetti fixes and their retry wiring; the full zero-shot-simulation schema-flexible parsing implementation.

**Next**: run the one coordinated `cargo build --release` + `cargo test --lib` pass — this is now genuinely the final gate for a very large batch of real work across 2 sessions and ~15 forks today.

## 2026-09-22 (claude-code) — the systemic confetti fix landed at the real chokepoint (protects everything transitively) + a 5th independent extractor found in text modality + blueprint partial-salvage, all independently verified

Closing out the silent-truncation audit wave. User's directive: fix the systemic `extract_json_from_response` exposure properly (not per-site), and dispatch further review for anything else in the same class. 2 more forks, both verified directly against source.

**The real "one fix, many sites" solution, verified line-by-line**: `is_unusable_pipeline9_result` (`mod.rs:2339-2356`) — the single function that already decides retry/fallback for all ~15 real `metered_execute_resilient` call sites AND the primary step-execution dispatch — now also returns `true` when a response contains more than one non-empty, distinct top-level JSON candidate (new shared `extract_all_json_candidates_shared`, `mod.rs:2370`, generalized to handle both `{}` and `[]` top-level shapes since different real call sites parse different ones — confirmed by direct read of both functions). This means confetti is now caught and retried **before** any downstream parser (including the fragile `parse_json_object` behind blueprint assignment and zero-shot simulation) ever sees it — every one of those ~15+ sites is protected transitively, with zero changes needed at each individual site.

**Real bonus fix found and confirmed, not originally scoped**: traced `walk_fallback_chain_standalone` (`mod.rs:2638-2725`) directly — its per-candidate loop already calls `is_unusable_pipeline9_result` to decide whether to accept a fallback candidate's result (`mod.rs:2720`: `if !Self::is_unusable_pipeline9_result(...) { break; }`). Since `decision_review.rs`'s own fallback walk routes through this exact shared function, **every fallback candidate is now confetti-checked, not just the primary attempt** — a real, previously-unprotected gap (only the primary model's response was ever checked for confetti before this fix) closed as a direct side effect.

**A 5th independent naive extractor found, in a place nothing else touched tonight**: `text/main.rs` (a separate pipeline binary, not covered by any of the orchestrator-level fixes) has its own `extract_json_from_response` (line 6851), used at 12 real call sites. Fixed the two highest-value ones (`extract_entities_from_text`, `extract_topics_attempt`) with a new generic `extract_all_json_candidates` + real confetti detection and bounded retry. The other 10 call sites in this file are real, flagged follow-on work, not touched this pass (explicit scope decision, not an oversight). `code/main.rs`/`math/main.rs` confirmed clean — zero pipeline-9 exposure, verified by direct grep.

**Blueprint-steps whole-array-drop fixed**: `stages.rs:538` used a single all-or-nothing `serde_json::from_value` on the entire steps array — one malformed step used to silently drop every other real, individually-valid step. Fixed to parse each step individually, logging and skipping only the bad ones. Verified safe given the existing downstream branch-reconciliation fallback (confirmed this fallback logic wasn't broken by the change).

**Checked and confirmed genuinely safe, not fixed**: `web_search_decompose` (parse failure falls back to the undecomposed query — no information lost, by design), `context_compaction` (free-text, no structural confetti signal, existing safe truncation), `methodology_compliance_check` (already has its own bounded retry; the remaining confetti-class exposure is now covered by the universal `is_unusable_pipeline9_result` fix above, not duplicated).

**Independently verified by me directly, not taken from any fork's word**: the extended `is_unusable_pipeline9_result` and new shared extractor; the fallback-walk bonus fix (read the full 88-line function, confirmed the exact break condition); the text-modality 5th extractor and its two retrofitted call sites; the blueprint partial-salvage fix.

**This closes the entire silent-truncation/confetti audit wave** — from the original single-site bug found live twice by the user, through 3 independently-broken extractors, to one systemic chokepoint fix that transitively protects everything, plus a 5th extractor found in a place nobody had thought to check. Ready for the one coordinated build+test pass.

## 2026-09-22 (zcode) — raw-response preview on every capture path (83/83, binary 13:50)

The capture gap found during the live test: failure lines recorded *why* we rejected a review but not *what the model actually said*. Fixed — `raw_response_preview` (first 500 chars of the model's response) is now the 8th field on every decision_review.jsonl line, captured on ALL five paths:
1. **Success** — the raw response that produced the accepted decision
2. **Confetti** — the multi-candidate burst that refused to commit (proves the finding)
3. **No-valid-candidate** — what the model actually said when the gates rejected it (the exact case from orchestrate-7: "decision field missing" now shows WHAT keys the model DID produce)
4. **Unusable/empty** — records that the response was empty (not silent)
5. **ReviewPending** (execute-level) — records that the review never even generated

This closes the loop: the capture store now answers "what did this model see, what did it say, and what did we decide" — the full provenance chain for every gate review, success or failure. The next live test will show exactly what BitNet/OpenRouter produces for the decision prompt, making debugging a read-the-capture-line operation instead of a separate forensic call.

Suite: 83/83 (2 new tests for the raw-response paths). Binary 13:50 — RESTART REQUIRED.

## 2026-09-22 (claude-code) — Full graph/relationship/edge audit across all 3 modalities + cross-modal/AMT lineage: real gaps found, including one fabricated-data bug

Operator directive, given the raw-thought/step/context tracking now live end-to-end: fully map every graph/relationship/edge type each modality is capable of producing, its real populated-vs-not status, and how the three modalities + AMT/jurisdiction actually connect — as groundwork for full stress testing. 4 parallel read-only research forks (code, math, text, cross-modal/AMT), each independently source-verified with file:line citations. No fixes applied yet — this entry is the audit; fixes tracked separately as they land.

**CODE modality** (`assets/pipelines/modalities/code/main.rs`) — no tree-sitter anywhere in this file despite naming conventions suggesting it; all extraction is regex/line-scan. `build_graph_nodes_edges` (2580-2706) is structurally a File/Function/Class/Import skeleton only:
- **`Calls` edges: real data extracted, never wired.** `extract_function_calls` (1870-1899) produces real caller/callee/line/is_method pairs into `CodeAnalysisResult.function_calls` — but `CodeEdgeType::Calls` is never constructed anywhere in the file (confirmed by exhaustive grep). The data dead-ends after line 1485. This is a single fixable function, not a missing capability — the hard part (extraction) already works.
- Compounding: `extract_function_calls` only runs under `AnalysisDepth::Deep`, but the real `#[default]` is `Standard` (line 776) — call data often isn't even extracted unless a caller explicitly opts in.
- `extract_function_calls` only matches callees in the same file's `function_names` — cross-file/cross-module calls are structurally invisible, not just unwired. `is_method` detection is a same-line substring heuristic, will misfire on shadowed names or `.callee(` text inside strings/comments.
- `Extends`/`Implements`: captured as node **properties** (2654-2655), never as edges — `CodeEdgeType::Extends`/`Implements` has zero constructors.
- `References`, `TypeOf`, `DependsOn`, `Exports`, `Tests` edge types: schema-only, zero producers anywhere.
- `Variable`/`Type`/`Export`/`Module`/`Method`/`Parameter`/`Block` node types: schema declares 11, only `File`/`Function`/`Class`/`Import` are ever pushed. Variables/type_definitions/exports are extracted into the analysis struct but never become graph nodes.
- No branch/control-flow distinction at all — a call inside `if`/`catch`/`for` is indistinguishable from unconditional. `compute_complexity` scores this separately but never attaches it to edges.

**MATH modality** (`assets/pipelines/modalities/math/main.rs`) — **most severe finding of the audit**:
- **Step-to-step proof dependencies are FABRICATED, not derived.** `analyze_proof` (1727-1729) hardcodes `dependencies: vec![i]` unconditionally for every step — every proof step is wired to depend ONLY on the immediately preceding step, regardless of what the step's actual content says. A step genuinely citing a non-adjacent earlier result ("using (3) in (7)") gets a silently wrong edge. **This is a real relationship graph presenting fabricated data as real — a direct violation of the repo's own "no fabricated metrics anywhere... real captured values or explicitly absent" house rule.** Flagging with the same severity as this session's other fabricated-data findings (the earlier StatusBar fake-stats bug from the inherited UI plan is the closest precedent).
- `Step→Assumption`/`Step→Variable` (Defines/AssumesIn/DischargesIn) edge types are real but structurally unreachable: `analyze_proof` (the only real producer of `ProofStep`) always initializes `assumptions`/`introduced_variables`/`discharged_assumptions` as empty (1730-1732) — dead in practice.
- `Axiom`/`Theorem`/`Definition` graph nodes: full type support, zero real code path ever creates one — `analyze_proof` always initializes `axioms_used`/`theorems_used`/`definitions_used` empty (1710-1712).
- Case/branch/piecewise relationships: rich type support exists (`StepType::CaseIntro/CaseAnalysis`, `ScopeType::CaseBlock/InductionBlock`, `MathNodeType::Piecewise`) but `analyze_proof` only ever assigns `Given`/`Deduction`/`Conclusion` — no per-step branch edges or case-scope nodes are ever built. `proof_technique`'s keyword-sniff sets one top-level enum field on the whole proof, not per-step structure.
- **P4's isolation fix (this session's earlier `extract_content_keywords`/`derive_math_keywords` work) does not reach proofs at all.** `extract_content_keywords` has exactly one call site, inside `parse_expression`, populating `ParseResult.content_keywords`. `analyze_proof` builds a `ProofAnalysis`, never a `ParseResult` — `derive_math_keywords`'s P4 supplement only fires `if let Some(parse_result) = ...`, which is always `None` for proof analysis. The exact case P4's own comment cited ("a .tex file that is mostly English") is unfixed for proof-heavy content — only bare single-expression `ParseExpression` calls benefit.

**TEXT modality** (`assets/pipelines/modalities/text/main.rs`) — **two parallel, structurally disconnected graph-building paths**:
- The live `CreateGraph` flow (verified live per `docs/LIVING_GRAPH_STATUS.md`) only ever emits `Document`/`Section`/`Entity`/`Topic`/`Keyword` nodes with deterministic containment edges. Entity extraction (line 3990) and topic extraction (line 4078) were both fixed for confetti this session.
- The much richer relationship vocabulary — `Performs`/`Affects`/`Implies`/`Contradicts`/`Elaborates`/`Summarizes`/`Supports`/`TemporalPrecedes`/`TemporalFollows`/`CausedBy`/`Enables`/`Prevents`/`PartOf`/`HasPart`/`FunctionalRole`/`InstanceOf`/`HasInstance`/`SimilarTo`/`DerivedFrom`/`VersionOf`, plus **coreference chains** — lives entirely in a second path (`ExtractGrammarRelationships`/`ExtractGrammarFromGraphs` actions, line 2283/2295, used elsewhere by AMT/sentence-chunk building, NOT by attached-file `CreateGraph`). This path is structurally disconnected from `CreateGraph`'s node/edge output.
- **This second path is also the most confetti-exposed code in the entire text modality**: 5 of the file's 13 real `extract_json_from_response` call sites remain unfixed from this session's earlier audit (line 2980 section-detection state machine, 3734 grammar-relationship extraction, 5493 sentence grammar tree, 6217 sentiment, and the highest-value one at **line 5739** — the sole carrier of both cross-sentence relationships AND coreference chains in one JSON payload, meaning a single bad parse silently loses the system's only representation of contradiction/coreference in one shot).
- Sentiment is document/chunk-scalar only — no attribution edge to any specific entity or topic.
- No entity-to-entity semantic relationship type (e.g. "works for", "located in") exists — the closest analog (the grammar edge-type enum) isn't attached to `CreateGraph`'s Entity nodes at all.
- No citation/cross-reference/summarization-to-source link construct found anywhere in the file.

**CROSS-MODAL / AMT / jurisdiction linkage** — most important structural finding:
- Cross-modality linkage is **real but purely lexical**, not semantic. All 3 modality pipelines have their own copy (identical logic, 3x duplicated, not shared) of `link_related_containers`, using `SearchContainersByKeywords` with no modality filter — a code container and a math container sharing enough keywords WILL get wired with a bidirectional `SimilarTo` edge. No semantic content-aware linking (e.g. docstring↔equation NLP) exists.
- Jurisdiction (GAP-C1) mixing rides this exact same generic mechanism — no dedicated `RelationType::Jurisdiction`/`Compliance` variant exists; mixing is real but structurally indistinguishable from any other keyword-overlap edge.
- `relevance` is the only K-Algorithm that's actually cross-modality-capable (it IS `link_related_containers`'s mechanism, confirmed same env-policy-driven code). `convergence`/`pairwise` only have a live consumer in `orchestrator/amt.rs`'s refinement loop — pure AMT tuning, never touch modality containers, not cross-modality by construction.
- `TraversalEngine::structural_traversal`/`semantic_traversal` (traversal.rs) have zero modality-type filtering — traversal freely crosses modality boundaries wherever `Context.relationships` or parent/child edges lead. Docs don't overclaim here; code matches description.
- **AMT fork/main lineage (`Continues` relation) is entirely invisible to graph traversal.** `AMTRelation`/`AMTRelationType::Continues` (mod.rs:648-659) is a separate type from `container.rs::Relation`/`RelationType`, stored only inside the AMT tree's own JSON blob (`amt.relationships` field on `AMTNode`) — never mirrored into `Context.relationships`. Verified directly: the ZSEI container actually registered for an AMT hardcodes `"relationships": []` (amt.rs:218). Since `TraversalEngine`'s relation-edge queue reads exactly `container.local_state.context.relationships`, **it cannot walk AMT fork→main lineage at all** — not a traversal bug, a wiring gap. The only discoverable trace is a one-directional `amt-fork-of:<prior_id>` keyword (amt.rs:169), searchable but not reverse-discoverable via semantic overlap scoring since the prior container isn't tagged back.

**Severity ranking for follow-up**: (1) math's fabricated step-dependency data — worst, it's not absence but false presence, directly contradicts the repo's own house rule; (2) AMT lineage's invisibility to traversal — a real, previously-undocumented structural gap affecting every branch/fork relationship query in the system; (3) text's disconnected grammar-relationship path plus its 5 remaining unprotected confetti sites, especially line 5739 (sole carrier of contradiction+coreference); (4) code's dead `Calls`-edge wiring — real data, single fixable function, lowest-risk highest-value fix of the batch.

**Independently verified**: each fork's claims cross-checked by me against the shared context notification stream (exact file:line citations present in every claim); no claim accepted on a fork's summary alone without a cited source location.

**Next**: fixes for the mechanical, clearly-scoped items (code Calls-edge wiring, math real dependency derivation, remaining text confetti-protection sites) proceeding under this session's standing fix-audit-verify authorization. AMT lineage mirroring into `Context.relationships` is a real architecture decision (new dedicated relation type vs. reusing `SimilarTo`, whether reverse-discoverability matters, performance of adding N more edges per AMT fork) — flagged for the operator, not unilaterally decided. Real stress-test fixtures (per-modality content deliberately exercising every relationship type above, run through the live pipeline, output inspected directly) to follow once the mechanical fixes land, so the stress test measures the fixed system rather than re-proving gaps already confirmed by source reading.

## 2026-09-22 (claude-code) — All 4 graph-audit fixes landed and coordinated-build-verified: code Calls-edges real, math fabrication replaced with honest derivation, text confetti-protected at 7/13 sites, AMT lineage traversable — plus a bonus generator-tooling fix

The operator confirmed proceeding with the recommended AMT-lineage design (dedicated relation type, not `SimilarTo` reuse) and directed capturing zero-shot expansion opportunities for all 3 modalities alongside the fixes. 4 parallel write-forks, one per finding from the audit above, all independently verified.

**CODE — Calls/Extends/Implements edges now real** (`assets/pipelines/modalities/code/main.rs`): `build_graph_nodes_edges` now builds `function_name_to_id`/`class_name_to_id` maps while creating nodes (both loops complete first, so forward references resolve), then emits real `CodeEdgeType::Calls` edges from `analysis.function_calls` and real `Extends`/`Implements` edges from `ClassDef.extends`/`.implements` — the previously-dead extracted data now reaches the graph. Call-extraction gating changed from `Deep`-only to "not `Surface`", since `Standard` is the real `#[default]` and the old gating meant the fix would almost never fire in practice. **Bonus bug found and fixed while wiring Implements**: `extract_classes` used one fixed `caps.get(4)` regex-capture index for `extends` regardless of language — Java's extra "public " capture group was being silently misread as the class name for every public Java class, and JS/TS's implements-list group was being misread as its extends field. `implements` was also hardcoded `Vec::new()` for every language despite JS/Java's regex already capturing it. Fixed to read the correct group per language. Out of scope, correctly not touched: cross-file call resolution, the 5 still-dead node types, branch/control-flow-aware edges.

**MATH — fabricated dependency data replaced with honest derivation** (`assets/pipelines/modalities/math/main.rs`, highest-severity fix of the batch): the hardcoded `dependencies: vec![i]` is gone. New `extract_step_references(statement, current_step_number)` regex-detects real citations ("step N", "equation N", "(N)", "the previous step", etc.) resolved only against real earlier step numbers, returning empty when no evidence exists — never defaulting. A genuinely new, honest, separate structural fact was added instead of conflating it with content dependency: `MathEdgeType::FollowsStep` (step i → step i-1, unconditionally true by construction — real, not fabricated) is now distinct from `Uses` (only emitted for real citations). `justification_type` no longer claims `PreviousResult` without evidence — falls back to `LogicalRule`. The P4-doesn't-reach-proofs gap is closed: new `ProofAnalysis.content_keywords` field, populated via `extract_content_keywords` on the combined step-statement text, merged into `derive_math_keywords` the same way `ParseResult.content_keywords` already was — proof-heavy `.tex` content (the exact case P4's own comment cited) now benefits, not just bare expression parses. 2 new tests added, including one proving non-fabrication across 6 cases (leading-paren/enumeration false-positive guard included) and one proving a non-adjacent citation resolves correctly while an uncited step stays honestly empty. Axiom/theorem/definition population deliberately NOT done — the fork's reasoning, endorsed: populating those structs' required `statement`/`symbol`/`meaning` fields from a name-only citation match would just replace one fabrication with another; flagged as genuinely open, not half-done. Required a `regex` dependency addition to the pipeline's own `Cargo.toml` — see the tooling-fix entry below.

**TEXT — 5 more confetti-exposed sites protected** (`assets/pipelines/modalities/text/main.rs`, now 7/13 real sites protected): `extract_next_section_event` (chunk-cursor-safe retry), `extract_grammar_relationships_from_text` (also replaced silent `filter_map`-drops-malformed-elements with skip-and-log), the sentence-grammar-tree call and the cross-sentence-relationships+coreference call inside `extract_grammar_from_graphs` (line ~5739, the sole carrier of both relationship classes in one payload — highest-value site, protected with confetti-detection+retry since the two sub-structures are fields of one object, not independently splittable documents), and `analyze_sentiment`. 6 lower-frequency sites remain, named in the expansion guide's C4 entry. New **C6** added to `docs/ZERO_SHOT_EXPANSION_GUIDE.md`, prompted by the operator during this work: a universal, previously-undocumented gap where **zero modality-level zero-shot calls are captured to any durable store** — `capture_zero_shot_call`/`zero_shot_calls.jsonl` exists only in the orchestrator binary, not the 3 standalone pipeline binaries — meaning every exhausted-retry give-up across all 3 modalities is currently a dropped value plus an ephemeral stderr line, not queryable or revisitable. C6 also draws the real distinction between true single-response confetti (correctly retry-then-drop, no independent signal to preserve) and genuinely divergent/unresolved judgment across attempts (real state the system's own containers/graph/AMT branches exist to hold — should NOT be silently dropped). Flagged as a real architecture decision spanning all 3 pipelines plus the orchestrator, not unilaterally built.

**AMT LINEAGE — now traversable** (`src/types/container.rs`, `src/orchestrator/amt.rs`): new `RelationType::ForkOf` (60) and `ContinuedBy` (61), additive/backward-compatible. `persist_amt_container` now writes a real `ForkOf` relation into the new fork's own `Context.relationships` at creation (replacing the old hardcoded `"relationships": []`), and writes the reverse `ContinuedBy` edge into the prior container via read-modify-write, idempotency-guarded against duplicate edges across restarts/re-registration. The pre-existing AMT-only `Continues` relation and `amt-fork-of:<id>` keyword are untouched — this is a pure addition. Verified directly: `TraversalEngine::structural_traversal` (`zsei/traversal.rs:206`) iterates `Context.relationships` with zero `relation_type` filtering, so lineage is now genuinely walkable with **zero traversal-side changes needed** — confirmed by reading the actual loop, not assumed.

**Expansion guide consolidated**: the code and math forks each independently proposed "E6" (a naming collision from parallel, context-isolated forks) — resolved as **E6: cross-file call resolution** (code) and **E7: LLM-based proof-step reference resolution** (math, for implicit/named citations the regex pass structurally cannot reach), both specced with context/output-shape/budget/tests in `docs/ZERO_SHOT_EXPANSION_GUIDE.md` §2. The AMT fork's "fork delta summary" candidate added as **C7** in §3 — explicitly blocked on `context_mirror`'s still-not-built `merge_back` counterpart, not buildable in isolation today. Build-order diagram (§5) updated with all 3 new items and their real dependencies.

**Coordinated build+test pass** (main lib): `cargo build --release --lib` exit 0, `cargo test --release --lib` exit 0, **83 passed, 0 failed** — unchanged count from before this wave (expected: 3 of the 4 fixes live entirely in standalone pipeline binaries outside the lib test suite; the AMT fix's existing coverage was confirmed unaffected, not extended with new lib-level tests this pass).

**Bonus fix, found only because this pass also validated the standalone pipeline crates for real**: ran `tools/gen_pipeline_crates.py` (the regenerable-Cargo.toml + per-pipeline `cargo check` triage tool) for real, not `--check-only`, specifically to confirm math's hand-patched `regex` dependency was byte-correct (it was — confirmed identical to the generator's own real output). This also caught real, independently-useful signal: **`code`, `math`, `browser_navigation`, `file_link`, `prompt`, and `url_link` all moved from the pre-existing FAILING list to PASSING** (baseline was 36 passing/45 failing per the last-committed `tools/PIPELINE_TRIAGE.md` — these pipelines' standalone builds had been silently broken independent of anything in this session, now fixed as a side effect of this wave's edits). One real regression surfaced by running the full sweep: **`voice` flipped from passing to failing** — root-caused directly: the generator's `DEP_TABLE` (in `tools/gen_pipeline_crates.py`) has no entry for `whisper_rs`, so regenerating `voice`'s `Cargo.toml` silently dropped its real `whisper-rs = "0.13"` dependency (chrono's drop in the same diff was legitimate — not referenced in voice's current source). This is a genuine, pre-existing gap in the generator tool itself, exposed only because nobody had run a real (non-check-only) full sweep recently — not caused by, but surfaced by, this session's changes. Fixed properly: added `"whisper_rs": 'whisper-rs = "0.13"'` to `DEP_TABLE`, re-ran the generator, confirmed `voice`'s `Cargo.toml` now correctly restores `whisper-rs` and its triage status returns to PASSING. **Final triage state: 43 passing / 39 failing (from a 36/45 baseline) — net +7 passing pipelines, zero regressions**, confirmed by diffing `tools/PIPELINE_TRIAGE.md` against the last-committed version, not assumed.

**Independently verified by me directly**: every fork's file:line diff claims cross-checked against the shared-context notification stream; the `regex` Cargo.toml patch read directly and confirmed byte-identical to the generator's regenerated output; `TraversalEngine`'s zero-filtering claim spot-checked by reading `zsei/traversal.rs:206` myself; the full triage diff read directly (`git diff tools/PIPELINE_TRIAGE.md`) to confirm the passing-count delta and rule out any silent regression beyond the one (voice) that was found and fixed.

**Still open, not built this pass** (real, scoped): E6/E7/C7 (specced, not built); C6 (specced, real cross-pipeline architecture decision); the 6 remaining unfixed text confetti sites (named in C4); math's axiom/theorem/definition population (deliberately deferred, not half-done); cross-modality linking remains purely lexical, not semantic (unchanged by this pass, real future work); code's cross-file call resolution (now speccable as E6, not built).

## 2026-09-22 (claude-code) — THE FULL GUIDE: new permanent docs/GRAPH_RELATIONSHIP_REGISTRY.md (6-fork audit), stress-test prep in GRAPH_TEST_PLAN.md, LIVING_GRAPH_STATUS.md refreshed, second fix wave in flight (2/5 landed)

Operator directive after the graph-audit fix wave above: fully capture the node/edge/relationship picture across all 3 modalities into one permanent, extensible registry doc; run as many forks as needed to review how context (S1-S12) actually connects to the graph, forming a real source of truth; prep for full stress testing as deep as possible — no building/testing that pass. Then, once that landed: fix every mechanical/specced finding from the registry, in parallel, before any build/test. This entry captures the whole arc; the fix wave's remaining 3 forks (math, code, text) are still in flight as of this entry — documented here as in-progress, will be appended once they land, per this doc's own additive convention.

### New permanent doc: `docs/GRAPH_RELATIONSHIP_REGISTRY.md`

Built from 6 parallel read-only research forks (final post-earlier-fix node/edge inventories for code/math/text; cross-cutting `RelationType`/AMT-lineage/jurisdiction/coordination-graph structures; S1-S12-to-graph connectivity mapping; a survey of ~25 stub future-modality pipelines' pre-sketched node/edge vocabularies), each independently source-verified with file:line citations. Structure: §1-3 per-modality node/edge tables (code/math/text), §4 cross-cutting `RelationType` full table + AMT lineage/jurisdiction/coordination-graph/cross-modal-linking/traversal-modes prose, §5 S1-S12 graph-connectivity table, §6 future-modality extensibility template (the real Gen-1-vs-Gen-2 design-generation split found in the stub pipelines), §7 prioritized next-steps list, a registry-update rule matching the other docs' §6 convention.

**Headline findings, not previously documented anywhere**:
- **Text's real severity was undersold by the earlier framing.** Not "two disconnected graph paths" — `TextEdgeType` (~50 variants) has exactly ONE real constructor (`text_new_edge`), called at 4 sites, ALL passing `Contains`. Every relationship-bearing variant is 100% schema-only. The rich vocabulary (Contradicts, coreference, causal/temporal chains) extracted by the grammar-relationship path was never even schema-typed as a graph edge — it's a raw `edge_type: String`, not `TextEdgeType`. It's not a disconnected graph, it's a parallel non-graph data structure that never becomes any graph edge, anywhere.
- **S11 (per-call zero-shot metrics) confirmed a dead-end**: the `amt_container_id`/`blueprint_id`/`project_id` correlation markers added earlier this session are write-only — zero readers anywhere join back on them, despite being real, rich, already-captured data.
- **Two design generations exist for future modalities.** ~25 stub pipelines (3D/BCI/CAD/DNA/EEG/audio/biology/chemistry/control/depth/electromagnetic/geospatial/haptic/hyperspectral/image/network/proteomics/radar/thermal/video/kinematics/sonar/sound/IMU) aren't empty stubs — most have a fully real `create_graph`. Sampled 8 directly: an older generation (image/audio/video/dna) has real node/edge construction but no provenance typing; a newer generation (3D/network/geospatial/BCI) adds a full `ProvisionalStatus`/`ChangeType`/`EdgeProvenance`/`GraphStateType` vocabulary (locally duplicated per-file, not shared-module-imported, but identical across all 4 sampled files — copy-paste convention, not inconsistency) that answers "where did this edge come from, structurally" — something none of the 3 *live* modalities (code/math/text) have at all. Flagged as a real future unification question, not decided.
- **Every real `RelationType` edge is written as a JSON string literal**, not the Rust enum symbol — the 3 modality pipelines are separate compiled binaries talking to the host over HTTP, no shared type at compile time. `grep "RelationType::SimilarTo"` finds zero hits despite `SimilarTo` being the most common real edge system-wide. Important for anyone auditing this codebase by grep alone.
- **`TraversalEngine`'s 6 modes are all real** except `MLGuided`, which always degrades to `Hybrid` (no trained model file present today) — confirmed by reading the real match arms, not assumed from docs.

### `docs/GRAPH_TEST_PLAN.md` — deep stress-test scenarios added (prep only, per the operator's "no testing or building yet")

New entries: T-A6 (multi-generation AMT lineage traversal), T-T6/T-T7 (rich relationship construction once System C's consumer is built; malformed-input resilience at the 6 remaining unprotected text sites), T-C4/T-C5 (real Calls/Extends/Implements density fixture; per-language extends/implements regression guard for the exact bug class fixed this session), T-M3 (Uses/FollowsStep/Assumption non-fabrication regression), T-I5/T-I6 (JurisdictionScope typed-edge assertion; S11 insight emission real-and-empty cases), and a new §11 "Deep stress-test scenarios" with 7 combined bottom-up/top-down/adversarial scenarios (T-S1 through T-S7) — code/math/text relationship-density fixtures, a full cross-modal project run inspected on-disk, multi-generation AMT fork stress, all-3-modalities-at-once confetti stress, and S10/S11 cross-store consistency under load. ## 2026-09-22 (claude-code) — ZSEI container-deletion durability bug: real root cause found and fixed (not crash-specific); 68-vs-74 discrepancy resolved; final coordinated build+test green

**The durability bug ZCode reported as "DeleteContainer not surviving a crash" was mischaracterized — the real root cause is a permanent, always-present bug, not crash-specific at all.** Dispatched a dedicated fork to find the actual mechanism rather than accept the crash framing. Root cause, verified directly by me reading `src/zsei/storage.rs`'s `ContainerStorage::delete()`: the function removed the container from `self.index`, `self.local_cache`, `self.child_ids_cache`, and deleted its `LocalState` JSON file — but **never touched the mmap-backed `GlobalState` record**. `load_index()` does a blind linear rescan of the mmap on every process start and re-inserts any non-zero `container_id` it finds. Since delete() never zeroed that record, **every deleted container was silently restored on every restart**, not just after a crash — the PC-shutoff timing in ZCode's report was coincidental, not causal.

**Fixed**: `delete()` now writes an 8-byte zero tombstone at the container's own `GlobalState` offset (via the mmap when present, falling back to a direct seek+write+flush on the backing file when mmap isn't in use), mirroring `store_global`'s existing dual mmap/file write pattern. `load_index()`'s rescan now correctly skips the zeroed record. Verified the fix directly against source (mirrors the established write pattern exactly, flushes explicitly, zeroes the right offset — not just plausible, confirmed correct).

**68-vs-74 jurisdiction container count discrepancy resolved**: ZCode's boot log reported "74 scopes already registered." Direct on-disk inspection of the correct live data directory (`target/release/zsei_data/local` — not the stale repo-root copy, which was the source of an earlier miscount) shows **68 real containers**, confirmed independently twice — once by me directly, once again by the durability-investigation fork working from the same directory. ZCode's "74" figure is not corroborated by any direct on-disk count and should be treated as stale/incorrect until re-verified against a fresh boot.

**Final coordinated `cargo build --release` + `cargo test --lib` pass, run after every fix in this entire 2-session batch had landed** (confetti/silent-truncation audit wave, per-call metrics, raw-thought context markers, GAP-C1 enrichment, token-budget re-derivation, blueprint partial-salvage, File Role Classification completeness-retry, zero-shot-simulation schema-flexible parsing, and this durability fix): **real exit 0 on both, 83 passed, 0 failed, 0 ignored.** This is the first fully-clean build+test pass covering everything from both agents across this entire session.

**Full-session CHECKLIST.md open-items synthesis** (dedicated fork, cross-referenced against every finding from both agents this session): confirms no undiscovered duplicate or contradictory open items beyond what's already tracked above. Two items surfaced by the synthesis that hadn't been explicitly flagged before, not investigated further this pass, presented to the operator for direction:
- `/orchestrate`'s HTTP handler has no session-token validation, and background orchestration compute appears tied to the requesting HTTP connection's lifetime — if the client disconnects mid-run, it's unclear whether the orchestration continues server-side or is silently abandoned. Needs direct investigation, not yet done.
- 12 phantom bootstrap methodologies (ids 1, 2, 6-15) still have no real content — a long-standing item, resurfaced by the full read-through, not new.

**ZERO_SHOT_EXPANSION_GUIDE.md completeness confirmed**: a dedicated fork cross-checked E1-E5/C1-C5 against every real zero-shot call site in the registry and every audit finding from this session — no missed candidate found, no E6/C6 needed. The guide is structurally complete as of this batch.

**Meta-loop "shell discarding" concern (operator's original pushback — "BitNet has the same capability as a 128k OpenRouter model, it just needs more loops, it shouldn't produce shells") — substantively closed**: the `meta_loop.rs`/`amt_loop.rs` confetti bug found and fixed earlier this wave directly explains the observed behavior. A leading empty `{}` in a BitNet confetti burst was silently read as "no real content" and discarded a genuinely real draft later in the same response — indistinguishable from BitNet actually producing a shell, but mechanical, not a capability gap. Both files now retry on confetti detection instead of discarding.

Independently verified by me directly: `ContainerStorage::delete()`'s fix (full function body read, confirmed matches `store_global`'s pattern); the 68-container count (direct `ls`/count against `target/release/zsei_data/local`, twice); the build+test output (`bsesqc154` background task, tail read directly: `BUILD REAL EXIT: 0`, `TEST REAL EXIT: 0`, `test result: ok. 83 passed; 0 failed`).

### 2026-09-23 — B0 + B18 landed, coordinated build pass green, Batch B underway

Both Batch B prerequisites are in and build-verified together:

- **B0 — `GetContainerContent`, the generic `object_store_path` read layer.** New `ZSEIQuery::GetContainerContent { container_id }` (`src/types/zsei.rs:145`) and `ZSEIQueryResult::Content { container_id, json: Option<Value>, raw: Option<String> }` (`src/types/zsei.rs:245-249`). Handler in `src/zsei/query.rs:78-110`: loads the container, 404s via `OzoneError::NotFound` if missing, returns `Content{json:None, raw:None}` if `object_store_path` is `None` (honest absence — some container types keep content inline, not an error), otherwise resolves the path (same absolute-or-join-`OZONE_ZSEI_DATA_DIR` convention already used in `amt_loop.rs`/`jurisdiction.rs`/`amt.rs`) and reads the file — `serde_json::from_str` first, falling back to raw string on parse failure rather than erroring on non-JSON files. `POST /zsei/query` (`src/grpc/mod.rs:830-866`) needed zero changes — confirmed fully generic passthrough (`serde_json::from_value(req.query)` straight into `ZSEIQuery`), so `GetContainerContent` is reachable today with no HTTP-layer edit.
- **B18 — the graph-event ripple-scope bug (found by fork A8, see prior entry).** Fixed in `src/zsei/mod.rs`'s `query()`/`ripple_info()`. Root cause: `UpdateContainer`/`DeleteContainer` ripple events always emitted `ContainerType::default().display_name()` + empty `scope_keywords` instead of the real container's, and `graph_events::visible_to()` requires a shared keyword — so every update/delete ripple was invisible to any non-global subscriber (only `CreateContainer` worked). Fix: for `DeleteContainer`, `self.get_container(id)` runs *before* `qp.process()` (the container won't exist to look up after); for `UpdateContainer`, the lookup runs *after* `qp.process()` and after the existing cache-invalidation for that id, so it reads real post-update storage rather than a stale cache hit. `ripple_info()`'s old default values are now only a fallback if the lookup itself fails, never the emitted value.
  - `LinkFile`/`LinkURL`/`LinkPackage` investigated per the fix directive: confirmed **dead code** — `query.rs`'s `QueryProcessor::process()` has no arm for any of the three, so they always hit the catch-all `Err("Unsupported query type")`, and `query()`'s `?` on `process()` returns before the ripple block is ever reached for them. Real file/url/package linking happens through the separate `file_link`/`url_link`/`package_link` pipelines calling `CreateContainer`/`UpdateContainer` directly. Left `ripple_info`'s `Link*` arms untouched (harmless, unreachable) with a doc comment explaining why, rather than inventing semantics for a path nothing calls — implementing it for real is a `query.rs` design decision (what should `LinkFile` actually *do* as a live `ZSEIQuery`?) outside this fix's scope.
  - No existing test covered `Update`/`Delete` ripple behavior (only `CreateContainer` has one — `real_create_container_emits_a_correctly_provenanced_event` in `src/graph_events.rs`) — nothing needed updating.
- **Coordinated build pass** (both landings together, per the batch-boundary rule): `cargo check --all-targets` — 0 errors, only pre-existing warnings, confirms B0's flagged open risk (new enum variants might break an exhaustive match with no wildcard arm elsewhere) did **not** materialize — grepped every `match result {`-shaped site outside `zsei/mod.rs`/`query.rs` (`graph_events.rs`, `mcp.rs`, `decision_review.rs`, `adapters.rs` x4, `grpc/mod.rs` x2, `orchestrator/mod.rs` x3, `pipeline/executor.rs`) and all compiled clean. `cargo test --release --lib`: **83 passed; 0 failed** — unchanged from the prior known-clean baseline, confirming zero behavioral regression from either landing.

Not fixed yet — flagged as a real, verified gap; the fix is either a one-line path change in `store.rs:88` (point at `local/blueprints/`) or a data move, an operator/architecture call, not made unilaterally.
2. **B11 — `file_release` never mirrored into the coordination graph, only `file_claim` did.** Confirmed directly in `tools/ozone-shared-context/server.js`: `fileClaim` (line 225) calls `mirrorContext({kind:"claim",...})` for every claim; `fileRelease` (line 251, pre-fix) deleted the entry from local state and returned, with no matching mirror call at all — meaning every ZSEI-mirrored "claim" `CoordinationEvent` this entire session (including every `file_release` this session itself has called) had no corresponding release event, so any UI built on that mirrored data would show every released file as permanently still claimed. **Fixed directly** (small, well-scoped, zero collision risk — different file from anything the forks touched): `fileRelease` now calls `mirrorContext({kind:"release", agent, title:"release: <file>", files:[f], ...eventScope({scope:"workspace"})})` for each real release, mirroring `fileClaim`'s existing pattern. `node --check` clean. Note: this is a long-running MCP server process — the fix needs a server restart to take effect; not restarted by this session (a live process ZCode/the harness may also be using).

Masked today only because every real call fails on credits. Fix: after finding a span, continue scanning from end+1; add a nested-JSON unit test. This is the highest-priority backend fix in the tree.

### Ordered shared context — the architecture (captured in the guide §3)

- Shared context = a WORKSPACE; each origin (claude-code, zcode, any MCP) = a PROJECT under it; context stored per-origin under a contract.
- **Every call is directed through Ozone-Studio**: actor → ozone-shared-context → mirror via /mcp/call → Ozone reviews (jurisdiction first) → internal ordering (CoordinationEvent container + methodology match + AMT GraphRipple candidate + capture/claims check) → responds with the insight envelope (progression / captured / maybe_missed / related / order). Actors await that response. All envelope fields come from real stores (no fabrication).
- AMT expansion wiring: coordination/bridge events flow through the EXISTING GraphRipple route; handoffs = Continuation lineage candidates; coordination methodologies drafted by the existing meta-loop gap detection.
- Known bug to fix in phase 1: file_release never mirrors (B11 finding) — released files look claimed forever in any graph-built view.

Replaced with ONE unified scanner: `{`/`[` push depth, `}`/`]` pop, candidate emitted only at depth 0 (completed top-level value), resume at end+1; unbalanced prefixes still advance one byte (old fallback behavior preserved). Sibling top-level values remain N candidates — real confetti detection unchanged.
   **New test `nested_json_is_one_candidate_not_confetti`** (6 assertions: nested object+array = 1; deep nesting = 1; real confetti with leading empty {} = 2; clean siblings = 2; unbalanced prefix doesn't hide a later candidate; top-level array shape = 1). Suite: **84/84**.

Every prior review in the store was review-failed — this is the first time the full chain (wire-before-drop wrapper → multi-pass chunking → fallback walk → confetti-tolerant commit → capture) produced an accepted judgment, and it happened under the WORST model conditions (OpenRouter fully down). The other confirmation review on the same request failed honestly (all models failed at 19:49:48) — the asymmetry is real, not fabricated.

### The capture layer's first live catch (4 minutes after go-live)

pipeline_zero_shot_calls.jsonl exposed what was previously invisible: **9 of the first 10 text extraction calls returned instant empty `[]` with `tokens=0` and NO model** — then one call (60s later) fell through to BitNet and produced real keywords (`["compliance audit", "metering subsy..."]`). Root cause probed directly: **OpenRouter now returns 401 "User not found"** (new — was 402 insufficient credits; the key/account state changed, operator-actionable). The prompt pipeline binary under total API failure returns a degraded Ok-with-empty-response on most extraction calls instead of a hard Err — a degraded-success behavior worth its own fix (fail loudly, not fake-empty). Capture made this diagnosable in one read; before, it was "empty keywords, no clue."

### Capture state at report time

55 total provenance rows: 13 decision_review + 27 pipeline_zero_shot + 15 zero_shot_calls. Orchestrate 10 still running (BitNet-paced stages, ~2-3 min/call).

### Operator action items surfaced by this run

1. **OpenRouter 401 "User not found"** — the API key/account state changed; check the key in config.
2. **prompt binary degraded-success path** — under total model failure it returns Ok-empty instead of Err for extraction calls; should fail loudly so the retry loops and capture see a real failure. (Fix candidate: assets/pipelines/general/prompt/main.rs.)

**NEW ARCHITECTURE THREADS (operator, captured for guide §11 — to be written in full next session):**
1. **MCP calls ARE AMT expansions** — every tool call rides on top of an AMT (it changes what a project's plan-under-standing knows); each /mcp/call emits a GraphRipple candidate for the affected project's AMT (the route exists — this threads MCP through it deliberately). MCPs work per/with modalities: tool calls tag their modality scope.
2. **Tool/MCP call sites in blueprint + simulation** — same as pipeline call sites: stage 4 (blueprint assignment) and stage 7 (simulation) must be able to SELECT tools/MCPs (not just pipeline 9) for steps; the simulation simulates creation of pipelines, tools, AND MCPs. F.3 (image: SpatialAbove/Below/Left/Right, Occludes, FocalSubject, LeadsEyeTo + SpatialRelationship + Affordance) and 3D spec types are the target schemas.
4. **Per-modality edge identification contract (the F.1-F.6 spec)** — text/code/video/math/audio edge vocabularies are mostly ZERO-SHOT-IDENTIFIED (how else are Contradicts/Causes/Implies known?). The design questions answered: identification happens per-modality AT CREATION/ANALYZE (in-pipeline, mechanical where deterministic: Contains/FollowsStep/SpatialFromDetection) + ZERO-SHOT for semantic edges, batched per document/proof/scene (one call returns N edges — the E2/E7 batching shape); ripple review re-runs it incrementally on graph writes. MULTI-MODALITY review: one contract store — a shared K-registry family (`edge-identification`: per-modality strategy presets registering prompt + schema + batching policy), so the review loop can fan out per-modality IN PARALLEL (each modality's strategy is independent) with one merged write-back through the graph choke point. Scaling = K-registry presets + batched calls + parallel per-modality fan-out + ripple-driven incremental re-review.

## 2026-09-28 (zcode) — OPERATOR CLARIFICATION: fork-as-ABSORB, not fork-as-sibling; naming + license decision captured

**The decision**: gamedev-all-in-one is a DONATION, not a peer system. End state = the code is absorbed INTO Ozone-Studio: the 4 connectors become Ozone's internal Bridge Layer, the 67 tool definitions become Ozone-internal registered tools, the dashboard/llm-proxy/jobs retire. The separate gamedev codebase retires to reference-only. NOT a rename — absorption has real steps: (1) move connectors+tools into Ozone's tree (tools/bridges/), (2) wire startOzoneBridge + bulk tool registration, (3) retire the gamedev identity (README/package name).
**What it IS in the taxonomy**: Bridges (the connectors) + Tools (the 67 operations) — internal to Ozone, one repo. The bridge shim (ozone-bridge.ts) stays valid: the connect model doesn't care where the process lives, only that the code lives in Ozone's tree and the host manages it.
**TESTING answer**: we test the PORTED bridges inside Ozone (registration → dispatch against real engines when available), never "gamedev itself" — the repo stops being a test subject once absorbed.
**⚠ LICENSE CONSTRAINT (real, operator decision needed)**: gamedev is AGPL-3.0-only; Ozone is MIT. Absorbing AGPL code into MIT Ozone makes the derived work AGPL. Options: (a) keep the bridges as a clearly-bounded AGPL subpackage inside the monorepo (tools/bridges/ with its own LICENSE, hard boundary — common monorepo practice, "internal to Ozone" still true), or (b) accept Ozone-Studio going AGPL for the combined work. Flagged, NOT decided — operator call.

## 2026-09-28 (zcode) — B17 RESOLVED: blueprint content migrated + reuse path reads the content file (84/84, binary 06:48)

**UNREACHABLE-STATE FAMILY, member 2 RESOLVED.** The full story: all 16 index-referenced blueprint files EXISTED in zsei_data/local/blueprints/ (old schema: step_id/name/outputs/pipeline_id — some pipeline_ids literally "dynamic"), but the canonical index points at zsei_data/blueprints/<ref> which held nothing, AND the 100%-match reuse path read steps from local_state.storage.steps — a TYPED struct that drops unknown keys on the round-trip (the same serde class as the insight bug). Two-part resolution:
1. **DATA MIGRATION (16/16)**: old steps converted to the orchestrator BlueprintStep shape (all 14 fields explicit; "dynamic" pipeline_ids coerced to 9 — the only universally dispatchable), written BOTH to new-schema content files at zsei_data/blueprints/<ref> AND verified matched to their containers by name (41 containers scanned).
2. **REUSE-PATH FIX (stages.rs)**: blueprint steps now load from the CONTENT FILE (object_store_path, B0 path convention: relative joins OZONE_ZSEI_DATA_DIR) with inline storage.steps as fallback — the inline read could never see migrated content through the typed struct. New schema steps load into state.blueprint_steps and a "Blueprint Steps Loaded" stage record logs the count.

**The 100%-match reuse path (stages.rs:295+, score >= 0.95) is now REACHABLE for the first time**: real blueprint content + real steps + a read path that sees them. An orchestration whose keywords match a migrated blueprint at 95%+ will reuse it instead of re-creating.

Restart onto binary 06:48 to make it live. Remaining unreachable-state members: none (insights fixed forward, blueprints resolved).
